\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
.

Proof.

Recall that the vector field
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
is timelike in
\{r_{+}<r<\bar{r}_{+}\}
, see Lemma
2.2
, and

(5.25)

\mathrm{K}^{W}[\psi]=0

for axisymmetric solutions
\partial_{\varphi}\psi=0
, see Lemma
5.1
. We apply the energy identity for the vector field
W
, see Proposition
4.1
, to obtain

(5.26)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{W}[\psi]n^{\mu}.\\

Now, by using the redshift estimate of Proposition
5.2
for
j=1
and (
5.26
) we obtain the first estimate of (
5.24
) by following arguments found in
[
16
]
.

To obtain the second estimate of (
5.24
) we use the now established first estimate, in conjunction with the twisted currents of Section
4.4
, in view of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

Note that the constants
C
on the RHS of (
5.24
) do not blow up in the limit
\mu^{2}_{KG}\rightarrow 0
.

5.4.
Boundedness in the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case

We have the following result in Schwarzschild–de Sitter

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
l>0
,
0<\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}<\frac{1}{27}
,
a=0
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, there exists a constant

(5.27)

C(M,l,\mu^{2}_{KG})>0

such that the following holds.

Let
\psi
be a solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
). Then,

(5.28)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

(5.29)

\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap\{t^{\star}\geq\tau_{2}\}}J^{\partial_{t}}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap\{t^{\star}\geq\tau_{2}\}}J^{\partial_{t}}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

for any
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. We can also obtain higher order versions of the estimates (
5.28
), (
5.29
), for any order of derivatives, by commuting the Klein–Gordon equation with
\partial_{t},N
.

Proof.

The estimate (
5.29
) is easy to prove, by following the same steps as in the lecture notes
[
16
]
(cf. the proof of Proposition
5.3
above).

To obtain the estimate of (
5.28
) we use the now established first estimate, in conjunction with the twisted currents of Section
4.4
, in view of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Note that the constants
C
on the RHS of (
5.28
), (
5.29
) do not blow up in the limit
\mu^{2}_{KG}\rightarrow 0
.

6.
Carter’s separation of variables

In this section we discuss Carter’s separation of variables, also see
[
6
,
35
]
.

6.1.
Sufficiently integrable functions

We define the class of sufficiently integrable functions, with the help of which we will make sense of Carter’s celebrated separation of variables of Section
6.5
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and let
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, we say that a smooth function

(6.1)

\Psi:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

is
sufficiently integrable
if for every
j\geq 1
we have

(6.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\Psi|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\Psi\right|^{2}\right)\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt

\displaystyle<\infty.

Note that if
\psi
is sufficiently integrable then
\left(\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}-\mu^{2}_{KG}\right)\psi
is also sufficiently integrable.

Remark 6.1
.

Note that Definition
6.1
depends on fixing
\mu_{KG}^{2}
. Specifically, in the case of the wave equation
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}=0
, we do not include the zeroth order term
\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\Psi|^{2}
on the right hand side of (
6.2
), as opposed to [Definition 5.1.1,
[
17
]
] . Therefore, in particular, in the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, constant functions belong to the space of sufficiently integrable functions as defined here.

6.2.
The class of outgoing functions

We will use the following definition in Proposition
6.2
.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. We say that a smooth function
\Psi
is
outgoing
if there exists an
\epsilon>0
such that
\Psi
vanishes in
\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{+}+\epsilon\}
and in
\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap\{\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}\}
for all
\tau\leq-\epsilon^{-1}
.

6.3.
Time cutoffs

Let
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. We choose smooth cut-offs that satisfy the following

(6.3)

\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(z)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\begin{cases}\text{1,}&\quad\{1+\tau_{1}\leq z\leq\tau_{2}-1\}\\
\text{0,}&\quad\{z\leq\tau_{1}\}\cup\{z\geq\tau_{2}\},\\
\end{cases}\qquad\chi_{\tau_{1}}(z)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\begin{cases}\text{1,}&\quad\{z\geq 1+\tau_{1}\}\\
\text{0,}&\quad\{z\leq\tau_{1}\}\\
\end{cases}

where the following hold
\partial_{\varphi}\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\equiv 0
,
\partial_{\varphi}\chi_{\tau_{1}}\equiv 0
and moreover
\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\big|_{(\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+1)}(s)=\chi(\tau_{1}+s)
, for some
\chi(s)
independent of
\tau_{1}
and moreover
\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\big|_{(\tau_{2}-1,\tau_{2})}=\bar{\chi}(\tau_{2}+s)
, for some
\bar{\chi}(s)
independent of
\tau_{2}
.

Note that for any
n\geq 1
there exists a constant
C(n)>0
such that for any
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
we have that
|\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}|+|\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}\chi_{\tau_{1}}|<C
.

We define the cut-offed functions respectively

(6.4)

\psi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(t^{\star})\psi(t^{\star},\cdot),\qquad\psi_{\tau_{1}}\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\chi_{\tau_{1}}(t^{\star})\psi(t^{\star},\cdot).

Remark 6.2
.

Let
\psi:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function. Then, we note that
\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\psi,\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
are outgoing, see Definition
6.2
and moreover we note that
\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\psi
is sufficiently integrable, but
\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
is not necessarily sufficiently integrable.

6.4.
The angular part of Carter’s separation, oblate spheroidal harmonics

We define the following

Definition 6.3
.

Let
f
be a complex valued function such that
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2},\mathbb{C})
and let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, for
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
we define the
L^{2}(\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi)
self-adjoint operator

(6.5)

\displaystyle P_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}(\xi)f\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}f)-\frac{\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\partial^{2}_{\varphi}f-\Xi\frac{\xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta f-2i\xi\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta\partial_{\varphi}f

\displaystyle+\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}a^{2}\sin^{2}{\theta}\cdot f,

where
\Delta_{\theta}=1+\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta,\penalty\ \Xi=1+\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}
, see Definition
2.4
.

By standard arguments for self-adjoint operators, see for example
[
35
]
, we have the following

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
and let
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, there exist countably many real eigenvalues

(6.6)

\lambda^{(\xi),\mu_{KG}}_{m\ell}\in\mathbb{R}

of the operator
P_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}(\xi)
, see Definition
6.3
, which are indexed by
\ell\geq|m|
.

Moreover, the eigenvalues (
6.6
) correspond to the oblate spheroidal harmonics

(6.7)

S^{(\xi),\mu_{KG}}_{m\ell}(\cos\theta)e^{im\varphi},

which form a complete orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi)
.

For brevity we will drop the explicit reference on
\mu^{2}_{KG}
, i.e. we will denote
\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell},\penalty\ S^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}
.

We need the following.

Lemma 6.1
.

The eigenvalues
\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}
of Proposition
6.1
satisfy the following inequalities.
If
m\xi\geq 0
then we have

(6.8)

\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\xi^{2}\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2},\quad\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\xi^{2}-2m\xi\Xi>0,\quad\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\xi^{2}-2m\xi\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}>0.

If
m\xi<0
then we have

(6.9)

\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\xi^{2}-2m\xi\Xi\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2},\quad\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\xi^{2}-2m\xi\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2}.

Finally, for any
(m,\xi)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{R}
we have that

(6.10)

\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}+\Xi\xi^{2}\geq\Xi m^{2}

Proof.

For simplicity, we prove the Lemma for
\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}=0
. Note that the same arguments work for
\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}>0
. For brevity define

(6.11)

f=S^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}(\xi,\cos\theta)e^{im\varphi},

and note that
\mskip 2.0mu|f\mskip 2.0mu|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}>0.

First, we have the following

(6.12)

\displaystyle P(\xi)f+\xi^{2}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}f)+\xi^{2}\left(1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta\right)f+\frac{\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}m^{2}f+2\xi m\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta.

First, we study the case
m\xi\geq 0
. We obtain the first inequality of (
6.8
) from (
6.12
) since

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(P(\xi)+\xi^{2})|f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Delta_{\theta}|\partial_{\theta}f|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\xi^{2}\left(1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta\right)+\frac{\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}m^{2}+2\xi m\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta\right)|f|^{2}\geq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}m^{2}\Xi^{2}|f|^{2}

where we used that
\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}\geq 1
. To obtain the remaining inequalities of (
6.8
) we proceed as follows. We rewrite the above (
6.12
) as

(6.14)

\displaystyle P(\xi)f+\xi^{2}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}f)+\left(\xi\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}\right)^{2}f+\left(\frac{\Xi}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}m\right)^{2}f-2\xi m\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}\frac{\Xi}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}f

\displaystyle+2\xi m\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta f+2\xi m\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}\frac{\Xi}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}f

and after some calculations we rewrite it again as

(6.15)

\displaystyle P(\xi)f+\xi^{2}f

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}f)+\left(\xi\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}-\frac{\Xi}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}}\frac{1}{\sin\theta}m\right)^{2}f

\displaystyle+2\xi m\Xi\left(1-\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}\sin\theta}\right)f.

Now, it is straightforward to prove that

(6.16)

-\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\cos^{2}\theta}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\theta}}\sin\theta}\geq 0.

Therefore, from (
6.15
) and (
6.12
) it is easy to conclude the remaining inequalities of (
6.8
).

Now, we study the case
m\xi<0
. We obtain (
6.9
) in a straightforard manner from (
6.12
), in view of the fact that
\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}<\frac{1}{4}
, see Lemma
3.2
.

Now, we proceed to prove (
6.10
). We write (
6.12
) as follows

(6.17)

\displaystyle(P(\xi)+\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\xi^{2})f=-\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}f)+\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\left(a\omega\sin\theta+m\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\frac{\cos^{2}\theta}{\sin\theta}\right)^{2}+\frac{m^{2}\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{1-\frac{1}{\Xi}\frac{a^{4}}{l^{4}}\cos^{4}\theta}{\sin^{2}\theta}f.

In view of the fact that
\frac{1-\frac{1}{\Xi}\frac{a^{4}}{l^{4}}\cos^{4}\theta}{\sin^{2}\theta}\geq 1
and that
\frac{\Xi}{\Delta_{\theta}}\geq 1
we conclude (
6.10
).
∎

We need the following definition

Definition 6.4
.

Let
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
denote the eigenvalues of the angular operator
P_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}
of Definition
6.3
, and
\omega
denote the time frequency from Carter’s separation, see Proposition
6.1
.

Then, we define the following

(6.18)

\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}.

We will often drop the dependence of
\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
on the frequencies and simply denote them as
\tilde{\lambda}
.

6.5.
The radial part of Carter’s separation

We here discuss the radial part of Carter’s celebrated separation of variables. For the first proof of the separation of variables see the works of Carter
[
6
,
8
,
9
]
. Also see
[
35
]
for Carter’s separation in the case of negative cosmological constant
\Lambda<0
.

We first define the Fourier transformations that will be used for the rest of the present Section

Definition 6.5
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
\psi
be a smooth sufficiently integrable function, see Definition
6.1
, where moreover the following holds
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
. Then, for
\omega\neq 0
we define the Fourier transforms as follows

(6.19)

\displaystyle\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}e^{-i\omega t}e^{im\varphi}S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)\cdot(-i\omega)^{-1}\partial_{t}\psi d\sigma dt,

\displaystyle F^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}e^{-i\omega t}e^{im\varphi}S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)\cdot(-i\omega)^{-1}\partial_{t}Fd\sigma dt,

with
d\sigma=\sin\theta dtd\theta d\varphi
. For all
m,\ell
the functions

(6.20)

\omega^{i_{1}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{2}}\left(\sqrt{\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}\right)^{i_{3}}\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r),\qquad\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},\qquad\omega^{i_{1}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{2}}\left(\sqrt{\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}\right)^{i_{3}}F^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}

extend to
L^{2}(d\omega)
functions for any
i_{1}\geq 1,\penalty\ i_{2}+i_{3}\geq 0
and moreover we have

(6.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}|\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}+\left|\omega^{i_{1}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{2}}\left(\sqrt{\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}\right)^{i_{3}}\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\right|^{2}\right)d\omega

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\left|\omega^{i_{1}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{2}}\left(\sqrt{\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}\right)^{i_{3}}F^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\right|^{2}d\omega

\displaystyle<\infty,

for any
j\geq 1
.

Remark 6.3
.

In the case of the wave equation
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we note that
\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
of Definition
6.5
is not necessarily an
L^{2}(d\omega)
function, in view of the requirement
i_{1}\geq 1
in (
6.20
).

Let
\Psi
be a sufficiently integrable function. We denote the Fourier transform with respect to the Boyer–Lindquist coordinate
\varphi
as follows

(6.22)

\mathcal{F}_{m}(\Psi)(t,r,\theta)=\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{-im\varphi}\Psi(t,r,\theta,\varphi)d\varphi.

Now we are ready to present Carter’s separation for the radial ode.

Proposition 6.2

(Carter’s separation,
[
6
,
8
,
9
,
17
]
)
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Let
\psi
be a sufficiently integrable function, see Definition
6.1
, that solves

(6.23)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\psi=F

where for the operator
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}
see (
1.7
). Let
\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},F^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
be the Fourier transforms of
\psi,F
respectively, see Definition
6.5
.

Then, for almost all
\omega
the rescaled function

(6.24)

u^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)

is smooth for all
m,\ell
and all
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
and moreover it satisfies Carter’s fixed frequency radial ode

(6.25)

[u^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r)]^{\prime\prime}+(\omega^{2}-V^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r))u^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r)=H^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r),\qquad H^{(a\omega)}_{ml}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}F\right)^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r),

with
{}^{\prime}=\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}
see Section
2.5
.

The potential
V
can we written as

(6.26)

V\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muV^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)=V_{\textit{SL}}(r)+\left(V_{0}\right)^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}(r),

where

(6.27)

\displaystyle V_{\textit{SL}}

\displaystyle=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{-1/2}\left(\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}\right)^{2}(\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}})

\displaystyle=-\Delta^{2}\frac{3r^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}}+\Delta\frac{-5\frac{r^{4}}{l^{2}}+3r^{2}(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})-4Mr+a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}},

\displaystyle V_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2}a^{2})-\Xi^{2}a^{2}m^{2}-2m\omega a\Xi(\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2}))}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2am\omega\Xi)+\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}.

Moreover, if
\psi
is in addition assumed outgoing, see Definition
6.2
, then the following boundary conditions hold

(6.28)

\displaystyle\frac{du}{dr^{\star}}=-i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u,\qquad\frac{du}{dr^{\star}}=i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u,

at
r^{\star}=-\infty,r^{\star}=+\infty
respectively, in the sense:

(6.29)

\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow-\infty}\left(u^{\prime}(r^{\star})+i(\omega-\omega_{+}m)u(r^{\star})\right)=0,\qquad\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow\infty}\left(u^{\prime}(r^{\star})-i(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m)u(r^{\star})\right)=0,

where
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\penalty\ \bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
. Finally, we note that
|u|^{2}(\pm\infty)=\lim_{r^{\star}\rightarrow\pm\infty}|u|^{2}(r^{\star})
are well defined.

6.6.
Parseval identities and inequalities

We have the following Parseval identities

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
.

Let
\Psi
be a sufficiently integrable function, see Definition
6.1
. Then, we have the following identities

(6.30)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{t}\Psi|^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\omega^{2}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m,\ell}(r)|^{2}d\omega,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi\cdot Y\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\cdot\overline{Y^{(a\omega)}_{m,\ell}}d\omega,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m,\ell}|^{2}d\omega=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|^{d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}}\nabla\Psi|_{d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}}^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt-a^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\partial_{t}\Psi|^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt,

where
{}^{d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}}\nabla
is the covariant derivative of
d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
is the standard metric of the unit sphere, see Section
2.10
. For
\lambda^{(\xi)}_{m\ell}
, with
\xi=a\omega
, see Proposition
6.1
. In the second identity of (
6.30
) we also assumed that the integrals are finite.

Furthermore, there exist constants
c(a,M,l),C(a,M,l)>0
, such that if
\Psi
is in addition an outgoing function, see Definition
6.2
, then for almost all
\omega
the radial functions
|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}(\pm\infty)
are well defined for all
m,\ell
, and we have the following identity

(6.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\varphi}\Psi|^{2}(r_{+},\theta,\varphi,t)\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}|m\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(-\infty)|^{2}d\omega.

Remark 6.4
.

We note that
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|^{d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}}\nabla\Psi|_{d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}}^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt\sim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\not{\nabla}\Psi|^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt
.

6.7.
Energy identity for outgoing solutions
u
of (
6.25
)

Note the following

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}\right)
. Let
u
be a smooth solution of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) which moreover satisfies the boundary conditions (
6.28
), in the sense of (
6.29
).

Then, we have the following

(6.32)

\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}(\infty)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)|u|^{2}(-\infty)=\imaginary(\bar{u}H).

Proof.

We multiply the radial ode (
6.25
) with
\bar{u}
and after integration by parts we obtain the desired result and the fact that we may rewrite the asymptotic formulas (
6.28
) as follows

(6.33)

u^{\prime}=-i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,\qquad u^{\prime}=i\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)u,

at
r^{\star}=-\infty,+\infty
respectively.
∎

6.8.
The Wronskian and representation formulas for the inhomogeneous Carter radial ode

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\penalty\ \omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
, where
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
. Moreover, let

(6.34)

(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},\qquad(u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}})^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}

simply denoted as
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},\penalty\ u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}
, be the solutions of the homogeneous radial ode

(6.35)

u^{\prime\prime}+(\omega^{2}-V)u=0,

that satisfy respectively the outgoing asymptotics

(6.36)

u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\sim(r-r_{+})^{\eta_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}},\qquad u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}\sim(\bar{r}_{+}-r)^{\eta_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}}

where

(6.37)

\eta_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=-\frac{i\left(\omega-\omega_{+}m\right)}{2\kappa_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}},\qquad\eta_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}=\frac{i\left(\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m\right)}{2\kappa_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}},

where note the surface gravities
\kappa_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\Big|(r_{+}),\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\kappa_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\Big|(\bar{r}_{+})
. For the existence of the solutions (
6.34
) see the book of Olver
[
47
]
.

Definition 6.6
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
, where moreover
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
. We define the Wronskian associated to the homogeneous radial ode (
6.35
) to be

(6.38)

\mathcal{W}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\omega,m,\ell)=\mathcal{W}[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}](\omega,m,\ell)=u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}^{\prime}u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}^{\prime},

where we note that the RHS of (
6.38
) is independent of
r^{\star}
.

Let
\omega\in\mathbb{R},\penalty\ m\in\mathbb{Z},\penalty\ \ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|},\penalty\ \omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\penalty\ \omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
. We have that a non-trivial real
(\omega,m,\ell)
–frequency mode solution of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) exists if and only if we have
\mathcal{W}[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}](\omega,m,\ell)=0
.

Remark 6.5
.

In our discussion above on real
(\omega,m,\ell)
-frequency mode solutions we have excluded the case
\omega=m=0
where in fact trivial mode solutions do exist in the case
\mu_{KG}=0
, and they correspond to constant solutions of the wave equation (
1.7
) (with
\mu_{KG}=0
). These frequencies are treated with the main estimate of Section
16.5.2
, namely Proposition
16.4
. Due to the existence of these constant solutions we have that in Proposition
16.4
we do not control the zeroth order term of the solution of Carter’s radial ode, but only control derivative terms of the solution of Carter’s radial ode.

Moreover, in the definition of the Wronskain, see Definition
6.6
, and in our discussion above on real
(\omega,m,\ell)
frequency mode solutions we have, more generally, excluded the frequencies
\omega\penalty\ =\penalty\ \omega_{+}m
,
\omega=\bar{\omega}_{+}m
, since we did not uniquely define the solutions
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},\penalty\ u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}
for those frequency cases. Furthermore, a benefit of excluding those frequencies is that in Proposition
6.5
we can prove that the Wronskian is a continuous function in
\omega
in a straightforward manner. Finally, we prove energy estimates for the bounded frequency regimes
\{0\leq|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\epsilon\}\cup\{0\leq|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\epsilon\}
, for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, see Proposition
16.6
.

We have the following proposition

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
, where moreover
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\penalty\ \omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
. Then, the Wronskian
\mathcal{W}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\cdot,m,\ell)
, see Definition
6.6
, is a continuous function in
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{\omega_{+}m,\bar{\omega}_{+}m\}
.

Proof.

This follows easily since we have that for
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m
and
\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
the solutions
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}
, see (
6.36
), depend continuously on
\omega
in the desired domain.
∎

Note the following Lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Let
(\omega,m,\ell)
with
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
be such that

(6.39)

|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty

where for the Wronskian see Definition
6.6
.

Then, for smooth solutions of Carter’s separation of variables, see Proposition
6.2
, that moreover satisfy the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), in the sense of (
6.29
), we obtain the following representation formula

(6.40)

u(r^{\star})=\mathcal{W}^{-1}\cdot\left(u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}^{+}}}(r^{\star})\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}}u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(x^{\star})H(x^{\star})dx^{\star}+u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(r^{\star})\int_{r^{\star}}^{+\infty}u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}^{+}}}(x^{\star})H(x^{\star})\right),

where the equality (
6.40
) holds pointwise; for
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}
see (
6.36
).

Proof.

Follow verbatim the arguments of Shlapentokh-Rothman [
[
54
]
, Section 3], which rely on a classic variation of parameters argument.
∎

Moreover, we have the following on Schwarzschild–de Sitter

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
l>0
,
(0,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then, for any
\omega\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},\penalty\ m\in\mathbb{Z},\penalty\ \ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
we have that
\mathcal{W}(\omega,m,\ell)\neq 0
.

Proof.

From Proposition
6.4
we have that

(6.41)

\omega|u|^{2}(\infty)+\omega|u|^{2}(-\infty)=0,

where
u
is a smooth solution of Carter’s homogeneous radial ode (
6.25
), from which it follows easily that
u\equiv 0
.
∎

6.9.
The operator
\mathcal{P}_{trap}

Let
\Psi
be a sufficiently integrable function, see Definition
6.1
. We define the following operator

(6.42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\Psi]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\left|1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right|e^{i\omega t}\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{-im\phi}d\omega,

where
r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in(R^{-},R^{+})\cup\{0\}\subsetneq(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})\cup\{0\}
, see already Theorem
1
and Theorem
8.3
.

Furthermore, we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\Psi]:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\left|1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right|e^{i\omega t}i\omega\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{-im\phi}d\omega,

\displaystyle|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\Psi]|:=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\left|1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right|e^{i\omega t}\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)\not{\nabla}\left(S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)e^{-im\phi}\right)d\omega\right|.

We record a global Parseval identity and an estimate

(6.44)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{trap}[\Psi]|^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dr^{\star}dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\omega^{2}\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}dr^{\star}d\omega,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{trap}[\Psi]|^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dr^{\star}dt

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\omega^{2}\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}dr^{\star}d\omega

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}dr^{\star}d\omega

also see the Section
6.6
on Parseval identities.

Since
\Psi
is sufficiently integrable we have that

(6.45)

\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}|\mathcal{P}_{trap}[\Psi]|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{trap}[\Psi]|^{2}\right)<\infty.

6.10.
The superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}

We need the following

Definition 6.7
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. We define the superradiant frequencies as

(6.46)

\mathcal{SF}=\{(\omega,m):\penalty\ am\omega\in\left(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\}

or equivalently
\mathcal{SF}=\{(\omega,m):\penalty\ \left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)<0\}
.

In the Kerr limit (
\Lambda=0
), in view of the fact that
\bar{r}_{+}(\Lambda=0)=\infty
, see Lemma
3.2
, we obtain that the superradiant frequencies are

(6.47)

am\omega\in\left(0,\frac{am^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right),\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{or equivalently}\penalty\ \penalty\ \omega\left(\omega-\frac{am}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)<0,

see
[
17
]
.

6.11.
The frequency set
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let
\mathcal{C}>0
. We define the set

(6.48)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}=\Bigl\{(\omega,m,\ell):\penalty\ |\omega|\in[\mathcal{C}^{-1},\mathcal{C}],\penalty\ \tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\leq\mathcal{C},\penalty\ (\omega,m)\in\mathcal{SF},\penalty\ |\omega-\omega_{+}m|>\frac{1}{\mathcal{C}},\penalty\ |\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|>\frac{1}{\mathcal{C}}\Bigr\}.

For the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}
, see Definition
6.7
. Moreover, for
\tilde{\lambda}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
see Definition
6.4
.

6.12.
The operator
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}

Let
\Psi
be a sufficiently integrable function, see Definition
6.1
. Given
\mathcal{C}>0
, we define the Fourier projection
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
on bounded non-stationary superradiant frequencies

(6.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}\cdot e^{i\omega t}e^{-im\varphi}S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(\theta)\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},

where for
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.48
).

By using the Parseval identity (
6.31
) we also have that

(6.50)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]|^{2}dg

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\varphi}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]|^{2}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|m^{2}\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}(r^{\star}=-\infty)d\omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|\Psi^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}(r^{\star}=-\infty)d\omega

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]|^{2}dg,

where the similarities above are with respect to constants
C=C(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})
.

Again, since
\Psi
is sufficiently integrable we have

(6.51)

\sup_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}[\Psi]|^{2}\right)<\infty.

6.13.
The set
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}

In the following definition we define various sets we need throughout the paper and in particular in Proposition
6.7
:

Definition 6.8
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. We define the set

(6.52)

\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}=\{(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}:\forall(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}\setminus\{\omega=\omega_{+}m,\penalty\ \omega=\bar{\omega}_{+}m\},\penalty\ |\mathcal{W}^{-1}(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{KG},\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty\},

where
\omega_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\bar{\omega}_{+}=\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
.

We define the set

(6.53)

\mathcal{B}_{0,l}=\{(0,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}\}=\{(0,M):\penalty\ 0<\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}<\frac{1}{27}\}.

By Proposition
6.6
we have that
\mathcal{B}_{0,l}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
.

Remark 6.6
.

Note that if
(a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
then we have that
(-a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
, in view of the fact that
\mathcal{W}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG}^{2},\omega,m,\ell)=\mathcal{W}(-a,M,l,\mu_{KG}^{2},-\omega,m,\ell)
.

6.14.
Quantitative mode stability as a flux bound

The main result of the present Section is Proposition
6.7
, which is a quantitative version of mode stability.

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
and
(a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
. Let
\psi
be a sufficiently integrable and outgoing solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
).

Then, for any
\mathcal{C}>0
sufficiently large, there exists a constant
C(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\mathcal{C})>0
such that we have the following estimate

(6.54)

\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)\right|^{2}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where
1\leq\tau_{1}
.

Proof.

Given that
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
,
(a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
, we repeat the estimates of [
[
54
]
, Section 3], which we here only sketch. First, if
\psi
is a smooth solution of the Klein Gordon equation (
1.7
) then we have

(6.55)

\Box(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)=F=2\nabla\chi_{\tau_{1}}\cdot\nabla\psi+\psi\Box\chi_{\tau_{1}}.

Furthermore, we define
u=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
, and
H=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}(\rho^{2}F)^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
. We have the representation formula

(6.56)

u(r^{\star})=\mathcal{W}^{-1}\cdot\left(u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}^{+}}}(r^{\star})\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}}u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(x^{\star})H(x^{\star})dx^{\star}+u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(r^{\star})\int_{r^{\star}}^{+\infty}u_{\bar{\mathcal{H}^{+}}}(x^{\star})H(x^{\star})\right),

from Lemma
6.2
which recall holds for
\omega\in\mathbb{R},m\in\mathbb{Z},\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}
such that
|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\omega,m,\ell)|<\infty
and
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
. Now, in view of the continuity of the Wronskian, see Proposition
6.5
, we have that for any
\mathcal{C}>0
there exists a
C(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\mathcal{C})
such that for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
(which is a compact set) we have that

(6.57)

|\mathcal{W}^{-1}|\leq C(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\mathcal{C}).

For
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.48
). By using (
6.57
) then the representation formula (
6.56
), in view of Parseval identities, see Section
6.6
, implies the desired result.
∎

7.
The detailed version of Theorem
1

In this Section we present our main theorem.

7.1.
Boundedness and Morawetz estimate: Theorem
1

First, we need the following definition

Definition 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. We define the following set

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}

to be the connected component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
containing
\mathcal{B}_{0,l}
with the standard Euclidean topology, where for the sets
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}},\mathcal{B}_{0,l}
see Definition
6.8
.

The following Proposition follows immediately from Remark
6.6

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
. Then, we have that
(-a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
.

The boundedness and Morawetz estimates read as follows

Theorem 1

(detailed version)
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
and

\displaystyle(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}},

where for
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
see (
7.1
).

Then, there exist constants

(7.2)

C=C(a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad R^{-}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0,\qquad R^{+}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0

where
r_{+}<R^{-}<R^{+}<\bar{r}_{+}
, such that if
\psi
satisfies the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) in
D(\tau_{1},\infty)
then we have the following estimates

(7.3)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{trap}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)|^{2}+|\partial_{t^{\star}}\mathcal{P}_{trap}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)|^{2}+\sum_{m}\left(1_{|m|>0}|\mathcal{F}_{m}\psi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

(7.4)

\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

(7.5)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]+|\psi|^{2}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

for all
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, where for
\mathcal{P}_{trap}
see (
6.42
), for
r_{trap}\in(R^{-},R^{+})\cup\{0\}
see Theorem
8.3
, and for
\mathcal{F}_{m}
see (
6.22
).

Proof.

See Section
10
.
∎

Remark 7.1
.

It is easy to see that in Theorem
1
we can replace the cut-off
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
with a general cut-off
\chi:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with the property

(7.6)

\supp\chi\cap\{t^{\star}<\tau_{1}\}=\emptyset.

Specifically, in our companion
[
43
]
we will use the cut-off
\chi=\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}}\chi^{2}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{1}+T^{2}}
where

(7.7)

\chi^{(T)}_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}(t^{\star})\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu=\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\begin{cases}\text{1,}&\quad\{\tau_{1}+T\leq t^{\star}\leq\tau_{2}-T\}\\
\text{0,}&\quad\{t^{\star}\leq\tau_{1}\}\cup\{t^{\star}\geq\tau_{2}\},\\
\end{cases}\qquad\eta^{(T)}_{\tau_{1}}(t^{\star})\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu=\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\begin{cases}\text{1,}&\quad\{t^{\star}\geq T+\tau_{1}\}\\
\text{0,}&\quad\{t^{\star}\leq\tau_{1}\},\\
\end{cases}

for some
T>0
. For notational ease Theorem
1
is proved with the cut-off
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
.

Remark 7.2
.

Given
\mu_{KG}^{2}\geq 0
, suppose that mode stability on the real axis holds for all subextremal black hole parameters, i.e.
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}=\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Then, since
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is connected, see Lemma
2.1
, we have
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}=\mathcal{B}_{l}
and thus Theorem
1
would hold for all subextremal parameters.

Remark 7.3
.

For fixed
(a,M,l)
, the constants in the RHS of the estimates (
7.3
), (
7.4
), blow up in the limit
\mu_{KG}\rightarrow 0
. Note, however, that
\mu_{KG}=0
is indeed allowed in the theorem, i.e. the constant for
\mu_{KG}=0
is finite. In contrast, the constants on the right hand side of the boundedness estimate (
7.5
) can be chosen uniformly for sufficiently small
\mu_{KG}^{2}>0
. Finally, it is clear from the steps of the proof of our Theorem
1
that if we allow zeroth order terms
|\psi|^{2}
ιn the hypersurface terms of the right hand side of estimates (
7.3
), (
7.4
), then the constants of these estimates can also be chosen uniformly for sufficiently small Klein–Gordon masses
\mu_{KG}^{2}>0
.

Remark 7.4
.

As discussed in the introduction, the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
includes the very slowly rotating black hole parameters
|a|\ll M,l
, where the smallness depends on
\mu^{2}_{KG}
. Moreover, the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
includes arbitrary black hole parameters
|a|<M\ll l
, and
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, where the constant implicit in
\ll
depends on the difference to extremality
M-|a|
, in view of the recent work of Hintz
[
26
]
.

Remark 7.5
.

The LHS of (
7.3
), in conjunction with the local estimates of Lemma
8.1
, also controls the spacetime integral

(7.8)

\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)\big(|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}+(\partial_{t^{\star}}\psi)^{2}\big)\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where

(7.9)

\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\left(1-1_{[R^{-},R^{+}]}\right)\left(1-\frac{\frac{R^{-}+R^{+}}{2}}{r}\right)^{2},

and for
R^{\pm}
see Corollary
8.2
.

Note that on a Schwarzschild–de Sitter background
a=0
we have

(7.10)

R^{-}=R^{+}=r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}=3M,\qquad\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\left(1-\frac{3M}{r}\right)^{2}.

Moreover, if the solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) is axisymmetric
\partial_{\varphi}\psi=0
, then (
7.8
) holds for
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
, where we have
R^{-}=R^{+}
.

By also utilizing the higher order redshift estimate of Proposition
5.2
, applied in the extended domain
D_{\delta}
, we obtain the following higher order Corollary, that also treats the inhomogeneous case

Corollary 1.1
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
1
be satisfied, where
\psi
is now a solution of the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
, where
F
is sufficiently regular. Then, for any
j\geq 1
there exists a sufficiently small
\delta(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},j)
such that the following hold. Let
\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}
be the extended spacelike hypersurfaces, defined in the extended domain
D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)
, see Definition
2.8
. Then, there exists a constant

(7.11)

C=C(j,a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0,

such that we obtain

(7.12)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\infty)}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{trap}(\chi\psi)|^{2}+\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)\setminus D(\tau_{1},\infty)}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}\left(|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\leq j}\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}|\partial_{t}^{i_{1}}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}^{i_{1}-1}F|+|\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}}\psi\cdot\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}-1}F|+|N^{i_{1}-1}F|^{2},

(7.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}\sum_{0\leq i\leq j-1}{J}^{N}_{\mu}[N^{i}\psi]n^{\mu}_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}\sum_{0\leq i\leq j-1}{J}^{N}_{\mu}[N^{i}\psi]n^{\mu}_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\leq j}\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}|\partial_{t}^{i_{1}}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}^{i_{1}-1}F|+|\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}}\psi\cdot\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}-1}F|+|N^{i_{1}-1}F|^{2},

(7.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}}|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}\leq j}\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}\epsilon^{\prime}|\partial_{t}^{i_{1}}\psi|^{2}+(\epsilon^{\prime})^{-1}|\partial_{t}^{i_{1}-1}F|^{2}+\epsilon^{\prime}|\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}}\psi|^{2}+(\epsilon^{\prime})^{-1}|\partial_{\varphi}^{i_{1}-1}F|+|N^{i_{1}-1}F|^{2},

for any
1\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and for any
0\leq\epsilon^{\prime}<1
, for some sufficiently small
\delta(j,a,M,l)
. Moreover, for the redshift vector field
N
see Proposition
5.1
.

Proof.

We first discuss the estimates (
7.12
), (
7.13
). The case
j=1
follows immediately from the consideration of the proof of Theorem
1
, where we now keep track of the inhomogeneity. Specifically, note that Proposition
6.7
also holds for the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation, with the additional term
\int\int_{D_{\delta}(\tau_{1},\infty)}|F|^{2}
on the RHS of (
6.54
). To prove the cases
j>1
we proceed as follows. We commute the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
with
\partial_{t},\partial_{\varphi},N
appropriately many times and use elliptic estimates (see for example the lecture notes
[
16
]
), where we also use the redshift Proposition
5.2
to control derivatives near the horizons.

Now, we discuss the boundedness estimate (
7.14
). Again, the
j=1
case follows from the considerations of the proof of Theorem
1
, where we keep track of the inhomogeneities. Specifically, we use Young’s inequalities on the error terms on the RHS of estimate (
7.12
) and folllow the steps of Section
11
, where we will now have appropriate inhomogeneity on the RHS of the estimates of Section
11
. We can obtain the higher order estimates by commuting with
\partial_{t},\partial_{\varphi},N
.
∎

7.2.
Logic of the proof of Theorem
1

We here present the steps of the proof of Theorem
1
, in an order that further clarifies the logic of the proof.

The first step is to prove the desired Morawetz estimate for sufficiently integrable solutions of the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation, valid for all subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black hole parameters
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
, but with an additional horizon term on the RHS, see Theorem
8.1
in Section
8
. To prove Corollary
8.2
we use the fixed frequency estimate of Theorem
8.3
, which is proved in Section
16
.

In the case where moreover
(a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
, we use the quantitative mode stability in the form of the flux bound of Proposition
6.7
, to remove the additional horizon term on the RHS, and we obtain Corollary
8.2
for ‘future integrable’ solutions of the homogeneous Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
).

In the
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
we use a continuity argument, see Section
9
, to prove that all solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) are in fact ‘future integrable’, see Theorem
9.1
in Section
9
.

In Section
10
we combine Corollary
8.2
and Theorem
9.1
to conclude the proof of the Morawetz estimate (
7.3
) of Theorem
1
, for
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
.

The remaining boundedness estimates (
7.4
), (
7.5
) are proved in Section
11
, where note we follow a similar approach to
[
17
]
.

For the axisymmetric case of Theorem
1
we do not need a continuity argument, and in fact the proofs are significantly simpler, see the proof in Section
12
. Specifically, by using the boundedness estimates for axisymmetric solutions of Proposition
5.3
, we conclude that axisymmetric solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation are in fact future integrable for all
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and therefore we can use directly the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
8.1
, which concludes the proof.

8.
A Morawetz estimate for sufficiently integrable functions

The main result of this Section is Theorem
8.1
. To obtain Theorem
8.1
we will sum the fixed frequency ode estimate of Theorem
8.3
, by using Proposition
6.2
and Proposition
6.3
, and we will also use the redshift estimates of Section
5
. (The proof of Theorem
8.3
is deferred to Section
16
.)

8.1.
Notation on constants

From this point onwards we use the constants

(8.1)

b(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad B(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,

to denote respectively potentially small and potentially large positive constants both depending only on the black hole parameters
a,M,l
, the Klein–Gordon mass
\mu^{2}_{KG}
. Specifically, we note that for certain inequalities the constants
b,B
will depend on the sufficiently large and sufficiently small parameters
r^{\star}_{\pm\infty}
, which will be determined later. Furthermore, for certain inequalities the constants
b,B
will depend on a sufficiently large
\mathcal{C}>0
constant. This dependence will be clearly denoted when used.

Informally, we record the following algebra of constants

(8.2)

B+B=B,\qquad B\cdot B=B,\qquad b+b=b,\qquad b\cdot b=b.

Moreover, let
f,g\geq 0
. When we use
f\sim g
we mean
bg\leq f\leq Bg
.

8.2.
The main Theorem
8.1

We have the following Theorem

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Let
\mathcal{C}>0
be sufficiently large. Let
\psi
be a sufficiently integrable and outgoing function, see Definitions
6.1
,
6.2
, satisfying the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
.

Then, we have the following

(8.3)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(-\infty,+\infty)}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\psi]|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\psi]|^{2}+\sum_{m}1_{|m|>0}|\mathcal{F}_{m}(\psi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C})\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\psi)\right|^{2}+B(\mathcal{C})\int\int_{D(-\infty,+\infty)}|\partial_{t}\psi\cdot F|+|\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot F|+|F|^{2},

where for
\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}},\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.42
), (
6.49
) respectively, and for
\mathcal{F}_{m}
see (
6.22
).

Proof.

See Section
8.4
. We will need first the fixed frequency ode estimate of Theorem
8.3
, to be proven in Section
8.3
below.
∎

8.3.
The main fixed frequency ode estimate

In the proof of our main Theorem
8.1
we will need to appeal to the following fixed frequency integrated estimate for smooth solutions of Carter’s radial ode that satisfy outgoing boundary conditions.

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
l>0

(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, there exist constants

(8.4)

\displaystyle E=E(a,M,l,\mu_{\textit{KG}})>0,\qquad r_{+}<R^{-}<R^{+}<\bar{r}_{+},

and a frequency dependent
r
-value

(8.5)

r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in(R^{-},R^{+})\cup\{0\},

such that for all
r_{-\infty}
sufficiently close to
r_{+}
and for all
r_{+\infty}
sufficiently close to
\bar{r}_{+}
, and for any sufficiently large
\mathcal{C}>0
there exists a constant
B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})
, as in Section
8.1
, such that for smooth solutions

(8.6)

u(\omega,m,\ell,r),

of Carter’s inhomogeneous radial ode, see (
6.25
), that moreover satisfy the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
) we have the following integrated estimate

(8.7)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{\infty}}\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+1_{\{m>0\}}\Delta|\Psi|^{2}+1_{\{|m|>0\}}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}+\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}+1_{\{|m|>0\}}\right)|u|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\cdot 1_{\{m>0\}}\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}|u^{\prime}H|+|\omega\cdot uH|+|m\cdot uH|+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\cdot 1_{\{m>0\}}\int_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}^{\infty}|u^{\prime}H|+|\omega\cdot uH|+|m\cdot uH|

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\frac{1}{\Delta}|H|^{2}

\displaystyle+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|\omega-\omega_{+}m||\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m||u|^{2}(-\infty),

where for
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.48
).

Proof.

Immediate by inspecting Theorem
16.1
.
∎

Remark 8.1
.

Note that the term in (
8.7
) that multiplies
E
in general does not have a sign. Therefore, in what follows we cannot replace
E
by a larger constant.

Remark 8.2
.

We only control the terms
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}1_{\{m>0\}}\Delta|\Psi|^{2}
on the left hand side of (
8.7
). The lack of control of this term comes from the lack of control of the respective term in Proposition
16.4
, for the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
.

Remark 8.3
.

We may replace the superradiant set
\mathcal{SF}
in the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
on the RHS of (
8.7
) with the smaller set found in
[
10
]
, for which real frequency mode stability is still not known.

We obtain immediately the following Corollary

Corollary 8.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, for any
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
and
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
and any
\ell\geq|m|
we have that

(8.8)

|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG},\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty.

Moreover, for

(8.9)

(\omega,m,\ell)\in\left(\{(\omega,m,\ell):\penalty\ \mathcal{C}^{-1}\leq|\omega|\leq\mathcal{C}\}\cap\{(\omega,m,\ell):\penalty\ \tilde{\lambda}\leq\mathcal{C}\}\right)^{c}\setminus\{\omega=\omega_{+}m,\omega=\bar{\omega}_{+}m\},

where
\mathcal{C}>0
is sufficiently large, we have that (
8.8
) again holds.

Furthermore, for
\mathcal{C}>0
sufficiently large then for

(8.10)

(\omega,m,\ell)\in\{0<|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{0<|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}

we again have that (
8.8
) holds.

Proof.

Let
(\omega,m)\in(\mathcal{SF})^{c}
and
\omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m
. Then, in view of the energy estimate of Proposition
6.4
we conclude the desired result (
8.8
).

Now, let
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\left(\{(\omega,m,\ell):\penalty\ \mathcal{C}^{-1}\leq|\omega|\leq\mathcal{C}\}\cap\{(\omega,m,\ell):\penalty\ \tilde{\lambda}\leq\mathcal{C}\}\right)^{c}\setminus\{\omega=\omega_{+}m,\omega=\bar{\omega}_{+}m\}
, for any sufficiently large
\mathcal{C}>0
. Then, in view of Theorem
8.3
we note that

(8.11)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{\infty}}\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}+\omega^{2}\right)|u|^{2}\right)=0,

for
H=0
, which immediately concludes that
u\equiv 0
for the desired frequencies, which in turn concludes (
8.8
).

Now we study the frequencies

(8.12)

\{0<|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{0<|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}.

For the frequencies (
8.12
) we can use Theorem
8.3
for solutions
u
of the homogeneous Carter’s radial ode that satisfy the boundary conditions (
6.28
) and obtain the result (
8.7
) without the last boundary term on the RHS (since
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}\cap
(
8.12
)
=\emptyset
) and without the inhomogeneities. Therefore, we conclude that for the frequencies (
8.12
) we have
|\mathcal{W}^{-1}|<\infty
.
∎

8.4.
Applying Parseval and summing in the redshift estimates: Proof of Theorem
8.1

We are now ready to prove Theorem
8.1
.

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

We apply Carter’s separation of variables to the inhomogeneous pde

(8.13)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F.

In view of the assumption of the present Theorem, specifically sufficient integrability and outgoingness of
\psi
, then Proposition
6.2
guarantees that for almost all
\omega
the function
u^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}(r)
is smooth for all
m,\ell
and
r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
, and moreover the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
) are satisfied. Therefore, we may apply the fixed frequency integrated result of Theorem
8.3
, and then integrate with
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}
, to obtain

(8.14)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{\infty}}\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}+\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}+1_{\{|m|>0\}}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\Big(\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}1_{\{|m|>0\}}(|u^{\prime}H|+|\omega\cdot uH|+|m\cdot uH|)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\int_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}^{\infty}1_{\{|m|>0\}}(|u^{\prime}H|+|\omega\cdot uH|+|m\cdot uH|)\Big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\Delta}|H|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|u|^{2}(-\infty),

where in the above we used

(8.15)

H=(H)^{(a\omega)}_{ml}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}F\right)^{(a\omega)}_{ml}(r).

We begin by bounding the inhomogeneous terms on the RHS of (
8.14
). Specifically, let
C^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}
denote the inhomogeneous terms (the terms related to
H
) on the right hand side of inequality (
8.14
). It is a direct consequence of Young’s inequalities and Parseval’s identities that we have the following bound

(8.16)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}C^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{|m|>0,\ell}\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}\Delta\left(\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+m^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{|m|>0,\ell}\int_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}^{\infty}\Delta\left(\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+m^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})}{\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\Delta}|H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\left|\frac{1}{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi\left(\partial_{t}\psi\cdot H-\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot H+\partial_{t}\psi\cdot H-\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot H\right)\right|,

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{cut}>0
, where in the last line of (
8.16
) we used that
H=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}F\right)
. Note that it is significant that in (
8.14
) that term multiplying
E
appears in that non-signed form. In (
8.16
), in order to bound the term multiplying
E
, we used the second Parseval’s identity of (
6.30
).

Now, in view of the inequality

(8.17)

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\sum_{m\ell}1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|u|^{2}(-\infty)\leq B\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\psi)\right|^{2},

where for the Fourier projection on bounded non-stationary superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.49
), we combine (
8.14
) and the bound of the cut-off terms (
8.16
) to obtain that

(8.18)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{m,\ell}\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+1_{|m|>0}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})\left(1-\frac{r_{trap}}{r}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}+\left(\mu^{2}_{KG}+1_{\{|m|>0\}}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{|m|>0,\ell}\int_{-\infty}^{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}\Delta\left(\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+m^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\omega\sum_{|m|>0,\ell}\int_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}^{\infty}\Delta\left(\omega^{2}|u|^{2}+m^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})}{\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{m,\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\Delta}|H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\left|\frac{1}{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta d\theta d\varphi\left(\partial_{t}\psi\cdot H-\frac{a\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\psi_{\tau_{1}}\cdot H+\partial_{t}\psi\cdot H-\frac{a\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot H\right)\right|

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\cdot\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\psi)\right|^{2},

where for the sufficiently small constant
\epsilon_{\textit{cut}}
see the estimate (
8.16
). In the second to last line of (
8.18
) we used that
H=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3/2}}\left(\rho^{2}F\right)
.

Now, since
\Psi
is sufficiently integrable and outgoing, we can use a pigeonhole argument and we can find a sequence
\tau_{n}\rightarrow-\infty
such that

(8.19)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\Psi]n^{\mu}\leq\frac{C}{\tau_{n}}

for some constant
C(a,M,l,\Psi)
(Also, see already Lemma
11.1
where we present a similar result, for functions that vanish on the horizons.) We use the redshift estimate of Proposition
5.2
in the spacetime domain
D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})
to obtain

(8.20)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{r\leq r_{+}+\epsilon_{red}\}}J^{N}_{\mu}[\Psi]N^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{r\geq\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{red}\}}J^{N}_{\mu}[\Psi]N^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle+B\Big(\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{r_{+}+\epsilon_{red}\leq r\leq r_{+}+2\epsilon_{red}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{\bar{r}_{+}-2\epsilon_{red}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{red}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\Big)

\displaystyle+B\Big(\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{+}+2\epsilon_{red}\}}|F|^{2}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{n},\tau_{2})\cap\{\bar{r}_{+}-2\epsilon_{red}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}\}}|F|^{2}\Big)

Now, we sum (
8.20
) and (
8.18
), for
r_{-\infty}<r_{+}+\epsilon_{red}<\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{red}<r_{+\infty}
, we take
\tau_{n}\rightarrow-\infty
, and we conclude that the following holds

(8.21)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(-\infty,+\infty)}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+1_{|m|>0}|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(\mathcal{C},r_{\pm\infty})\int\int_{D(-\infty,\infty)}|\partial_{t}\psi\cdot F|+|\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot F|+|F|^{2},

after taking
\epsilon_{cut}>0
sufficiently small. For the operator
\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}
see (
6.42
).

We have concluded the result of Theorem
8.1
.
∎

8.5.
Finite in time energy estimates

Note the following Lemma

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Given
0\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}<+\infty
, there exists a constant

(8.22)

C(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1},a,M,l)>0

such that for any solution
\psi
of the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
, where
F
is a smooth function, then the following hold

(8.23)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1},a,M,l)\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}|F|^{2}\right),

(8.24)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}|\psi|^{2}\leq C(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1},a,M,l)\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}|\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}|F|^{2}\right).

Proof.

This is a standard proof.

To prove (
8.23
) we use divergence theorem, see Proposition
4.1
, with a
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
multiplier in conjunction with an
N
-redshift multiplier, see Proposition
5.2
. Then, we use Grönwall and conclude.

To prove (
8.24
) we use Grönwall’s inequality in view of the direct application of fundamental theorem of calculus

(8.25)

|\psi|^{2}(\tau_{2},r,\theta,\varphi)\leq C(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}|\partial_{t}\psi|^{2}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)d\tau+|\psi|^{2}(\tau_{1},r,\theta,\varphi)

and conclude by also using (
8.23
).
∎

8.6.
Corollary for future integrable homogeneous solutions for parameters in the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}

In the following Corollary
8.2
we restrict the assumptions of Theorem
8.1
, and study ‘future integrable’ solutions of the homogeneous Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
).

First, we define the ‘future integrable’ class of solutions:

Definition 8.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Moreover, assume that
\psi
is a smooth solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) arising from smooth initial data on the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. We say that
\psi
is
future integrable
if for every
\tau_{1}>1
the function
\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
is sufficiently integrable, see Definition
6.1
. For the cut-off
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
see Section
6.3
.

We have the following

Corollary 8.2
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
and
(a,M)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
. Let
\psi
be a future integrable solution, see Definition
8.1
, of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) arising from smooth initial data on
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. Then, we have

(8.26)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},+\infty)}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

for any
0\leq\tau_{1}
, where for
\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}
see (
6.42
), and for
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
see Section
6
. For
\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
see Definition
6.8
.

Proof of Corollary
8.2
.

We cut off the solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) as follows

(8.27)

\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)-\mu^{2}_{KG}\cdot\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi=F

where
F=2\nabla\chi_{\tau_{1}}\cdot\nabla\psi+\psi\Box(\chi_{\tau_{1}})
.

The function
\psi
is future integrable and therefore
\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
sufficiently integrable and outgoing. We use the result of Theorem
8.1
for
\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
in the place of
\psi
to obtain the following

(8.28)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(-\infty,+\infty)}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)|^{2}+|Z^{\star}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi)\right|^{2}+B\int\int_{D(-\infty,+\infty)}|\partial_{t}\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi\cdot F|+|\partial_{\varphi}\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi\cdot F|+|F|^{2}.

Now, by using the bound of Proposition
6.7
, for the horizon term of (
8.28
), and the finite in time energy estimates of Lemma
8.1
,
we obtain the desired result (
8.26
).
∎

9.
The continuity argument

The main result of this Section is the following

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then, if
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
, see (
7.1
), then the solution
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) that arises from smooth initial data
(\psi_{0},\psi_{1})
is future integrable, see Definition
8.1
.

Proof.

See Section
9.5
.
∎

Remark 9.1
.

We will use Theorem
9.1
in the next Section
10
. If the solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) is axisymmetric
\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\psi=0
then we do not need the continuity argument of the present Section.

We use the generic constants of Section
8.1
, with the algebra of constants discussed there, but we will also use the notation
B(m)
to denote that the constant
B
additionally depends on the azimuthal frequency
m
.

Before presenting and proving our main results we need several preparatory Lemmata and Propositions regarding alternative trapping parameters for fixed azimuthal frequency
m
.

9.1.
The causal vector field
W_{mod}

The Lemma of this Section will be used later in the fixed azimuthal frequency Proposition
9.1
.

Let
\epsilon_{trap}>0
be sufficiently small, as chosen in Lemma
16.13
. We now modify the timelike vector field
W
of Lemma
2.2
to make it Killing in the sufficiently small region

(9.1)

\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}.

We state this result in the following Lemma

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Moreover, let
\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}>0
of Lemma
16.13
be sufficiently small.

Then, there exists a causal vector field

(9.2)

W_{\textit{mod}}

such that

(9.3)

\displaystyle W_{\textit{mod}}

\displaystyle=W,\qquad\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-3\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}\cup\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+3\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}\},

\displaystyle W_{\textit{mod}}

\displaystyle\text{ is timelike in }\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-3\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}\cup\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+3\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\},

\displaystyle W_{\textit{mod}}

\displaystyle\text{ is Killing and timelike in }\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\},

where
W=\partial_{t}+\frac{a\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\partial_{\varphi}
, see Lemma
2.2
. Moreover, we have the following pointwise estimate

(9.4)

|\mathrm{K}^{W_{mod}}[\psi]|\lesssim\Delta\cdot 1_{\{r_{+}\leq r\leq\bar{r}_{+}\}\setminus\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}}\cdot\left(\epsilon|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\epsilon(\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi)^{2}+\epsilon^{-1}|\partial_{\phi}\psi|^{2}\right),

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, where
r_{\Delta,frac}
is the unique critical point of
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
, see Lemma
3.3
.

Proof.

The proof of (
9.3
) is straightforward by considering a vector field of the form

(9.5)

W_{mod}=\partial_{t}+f_{mod}(r)\partial_{\varphi},

where
f_{mod}(r)
is constant in
\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}
.

To prove (
9.4
) we note that the following holds pointwise

(9.6)

\mathrm{K}^{W_{mod}}=2\frac{\Delta}{\rho^{2}}\mathbb{T}(\nabla f,\partial_{\phi})[\psi]

and conclude by using Young’s inequality.
∎

9.2.
Morawetz estimate for fixed azimuthal frequency
m

Note the following Proposition, which
does not
assume future integrability.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. For any
j\geq 1
and
|m|\geq 0
there exists a constant

(9.7)

B=B(m,j,a,M,l,\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}})>0,

and there exists a smooth function

(9.8)

\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)

with

(9.9)

\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\begin{cases}1,\qquad\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}\cup\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}\}\\
0,\qquad\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\},\end{cases}

where for the sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}>0
see Lemma
9.1
, such that for any smooth solution
\psi
of Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) with fixed azimuthal frequency

(9.10)

|m|>0

we obtain

(9.11)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

and

(9.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(m,j)\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[r_{+},r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}})}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}]}\right)|\partial_{\varphi}\psi|^{2}

for all
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
. The value
r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}
is the unique critical point of
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
, see Lemma
3.3
.

Moreover, in the case of future integrable solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation we can drop the two last bulk terms from the RHS of (
9.11
).

Furthermore, for
the axisymmetric case
m=0
we replace
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
with the entire subextremal family
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and moreover inequality (
9.11
) holds without the two last bulk terms from the RHS of (
9.11
).

Proof.

[formula]
for a fixed azimuthal frequency
|m|>0
. For any
\epsilon_{trap}>0
sufficiently small, we take
\omega_{high}(m,\epsilon_{trap}),\penalty\ \lambda_{low}^{-1}(\epsilon_{trap})
of Lemma
16.13
sufficiently large and in view of Lemma
9.1
we conclude

(9.13)

\supp\tilde{\zeta}_{trap}\subset(r_{\Delta,frac}-\epsilon_{trap},r_{\Delta,frac}+\epsilon_{trap}).

where
\tilde{\zeta}_{trap}
is smooth and

(9.14)

\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\begin{cases}1,\qquad\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}\cup\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}\}\\
0,\qquad\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}.\end{cases}

Given
\psi
we now apply the cut-off

(9.15)

\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}},

for
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, see Section
6.3
, in the place of
\chi_{\tau_{1}}
and repeat the arguments from the proof of Theorem
8.1
, which note holds for the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon equation
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=F
.

Specifically, we fix the constant

\mathcal{C}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0,

see Theorem
8.1
, and we obtain the following

(9.16)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(m,j)\cdot\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\left|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}(\chi_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\psi)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2},

for any
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
is a Fourier projection to bounded non-stationary superradiant frequencies, see (
6.49
). Note that to obtain the last estimate of (
9.16
) we used that

(9.17)

m^{2}|\psi|^{2}=(\partial_{\varphi}\psi)^{2}

for our fixed azimuthal frequency
|m|>0
in order to bound the future flux appropriately so that no zeroth order terms appear.

By commuting appropriately many times with
\partial_{t},N
, and by using elliptic estimates, we obtain the higher order statement

(9.18)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2},

which concludes (
9.11
) for solutions supported in the fixed azimuthal frequency
|m|>0
.

[formula]
for fixed azimuthal frequency
|m|=0
, i.e. for axisymmetric solutions
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation. By using Theorem
8.1
we have that for
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
(namely the entire subextremal parameter family without assuming mode stability) and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
then for axisymmetric solutions
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation the following holds

(9.19)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\left(1-\frac{r_{\Delta,frac}}{r}\right)^{2}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}
