\displaystyle\leq B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2},

where note we included the zero order terms on the future fluxes. Since
\psi
is axisymmetric we have

(9.20)

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}\psi=0,

see (
6.49
) for the definition of the operator
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
. Moreover, we note from the redshift Proposition for axisymmetric solutions, see Proposition
5.3
, that the following holds

(9.21)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}.

Therefore, by using the integrated estimate (
9.19
) and the bounds (
9.21
) we conclude that the following holds for axisymmetric solutions

(9.22)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\left(1-\frac{r_{\Delta,frac}}{r}\right)^{2}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}.

We have concluded (
9.11
) for
m=0
.

[formula]
. We use the
W_{mod}
multiplier of Lemma
9.1
, the redshift vector field
N
, in conjunction with the integrated estimate (
9.11
) and the standard redshift argument found in the lecture notes
[
16
]
to appropriately bound the
\dot{H}^{1}
future fluxes on the RHS of (
9.11
) as follows

(9.23)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(m,j)\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[r_{+},r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}})}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}]}\right)|\psi|^{2}

for all
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}

Therefore, we bound the RHS of the integrated estimate (
9.11
) by using the bound (
9.23
) on the future fluxes to obtain the desired inequality (
9.12
).

Finally, we note that if the solution
\psi
is future integrable, see Definition
8.1
, then we can use the cut-offed wave
\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi
, see Section
6.3
, and repeat the arguments of the present proof to obtain the estimate (
9.11
), without the future boundary term on the RHS.
∎

9.3.
Reduction to fixed azimuthal frequency
m

The main result Theorem
9.1
of the present Section will follow as a Corollary from the following fixed azimuthal frequency Proposition, which will be proved with a continuity argument.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let the assumptions of Theorem
9.1
hold, but moreover assume that
\psi=\psi_{m}
where
\psi_{m}
is the
m
-th azimuthal mode. Then,
\psi
is future integrable, see Definition
8.1
.

Proof.

See Section
9.4
.
∎

Note the following Lemma

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
l>0
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Moreover, let
a,M,\psi,m
be as in Proposition
9.2
. Then,
\psi
is future integrable if

(9.24)

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.71562pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}<+\infty,\qquad\forall j\geq 1.

Proof.

This is immediate in view of Proposition
9.1
.
∎

In the following definition, we define a set we need for the remainder of the present Section

Definition 9.1
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. We define

(9.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}

\displaystyle=\{(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}:\penalty\ \eqref{eq: cor: subsec: sec: continuity argument, subsec 0, cor 1, eq 1}\penalty\ \text{ holds for all solutions}\penalty\ \psi\penalty\ \text{of}\penalty\ \eqref{eq: kleingordon}\penalty\ \text{supported}

\displaystyle\text{on the fixed azimuthal frequency}\penalty\ m\penalty\ \text{with respect to}\penalty\ g_{a,M}\}.

For the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
see (
7.1
).

9.4.
Proof of Proposition
9.2

The proof of Proposition
9.2
will use a continuity argument. Note that the original continuity argument of
[
17
]
was a continuity argument only in the rotation parameter
a
for a fixed black hole mass
M
. In contrast to
[
17
]
the continuity argument of the present Section needs to take place in the space of parameters

(9.26)

\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}

which is by definition connected, see Section
7.1
.

First, we prove the following Proposition

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
l>0
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then,
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
is an open subset of
\mathcal{B}_{l}
.

Proof.

Now, let
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
. We recall from Definition
7.1
that the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
is the connected component of the set
\mathcal{B}_{0,l}
in the set

(9.27)

\widetilde{\mathcal{MS}}_{l,\mu_{KG}}=\{(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}:\forall(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|},\penalty\ \omega\neq\omega_{+}m,\penalty\ \omega\neq\bar{\omega}_{+}m,\penalty\ \penalty\ |\mathcal{W}^{-1}(a,M,l,\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty\}.

For
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small we obtain that if

(9.28)

|\mathring{a}-a|+|\mathring{M}-M|\leq\epsilon

then
(\mathring{a},\mathring{M})\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
.

For both the sufficiently large frequencies

(9.29)

\{\omega^{2}+m^{2}+\tilde{\lambda}\geq\mathcal{C}\},

and the frequencies

(9.30)

\{0<|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{0<|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}

we use Corollary
8.1
and we obtain

(9.31)

|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l,\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty.

For the remaining bounded frequencies

(9.32)

\{\omega^{2}+m^{2}+\tilde{\lambda}\leq\mathcal{C}\}\cap\left(\{|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\geq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\geq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\right).

we use that the Wronskian is a continuous function, on a compact domain, and conclude that indeed
|\mathcal{W}^{-1}(\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l,\omega,m,\ell)|<+\infty
holds, for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
.

We conclude
(\mathring{a},\mathring{M})\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
9.2

Proof of Proposition
9.2
.

Let
l>0
. In this Proposition we assume that

(9.33)

\psi\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\text{has fixed azimuthal frequency}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mum.

Specifically, we assume that the solution
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) arises from initial data

(9.34)

(\psi_{0},\psi_{1})

of fixed azimuthal frequency
m
.

First, we observe that for
m=0
that indeed the solution
\psi
of Klein–Gordon arising from
(\psi_{0},\psi_{1})
is future integrable, see Corollary
8.2
with
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}=0
and Lemma
9.2
.

Therefore,
in the rest of the proof we assume that
|m|>0
. We follow the analogous arguments of
[
17
]
.

First, we recall Lemma
9.2
where we proved that solutions
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) that satisfy

(9.35)

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}<+\infty,\qquad\forall j\geq 1,

are future integrable. We will use this to show that the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
is open and closed as a subset of
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
.

Now, we proceed with the clopen argument that will prove that

(9.36)

\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}=\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}},

for the former see Definition
9.1
and for the latter see Definition
7.1
.

To prove the non-emptyness
of the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
in the
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
topology we note that for all
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
we have

(9.37)

\mathcal{B}_{0,l}\subset\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m},

see Proposition
5.4
where we prove that for solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation in a Schwarzschild–de Sitter background the following boundedness estimate holds

(9.38)

\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}+\int_{\mathcal{H}_{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\right)J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}.

(Note that we do not need to use fixed azimuthal frequency.) For the set
\mathcal{B}_{0,l}
see Definition
6.8
.

To prove the closedness
of the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
in the
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
topology we follow the same arguments that proved an analogue result in [
[
17
]
, Section 11, Proposition 11.3.1]. However, in the present case we do not use a sequence of black hole rotations
a_{k}
for a fixed black hole mass
M
, but rather we consider a sequence

(9.39)

(a_{k},M_{k})\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}.

such that
(a_{k},M_{k})\rightarrow(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
. (This is because the subextremal black hole parameter space of Kerr–de Sitter pocesses one degree of freedom more than the subextremal black hole parameter space of Kerr.)

Let
\psi
be a fixed
m
-solution of
\Box_{g_{a,M,l}}\psi-\mu^{2}_{KG}\psi=0
. We define a sequence of functions

(9.40)

\psi_{(k)}

that solve the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) for the black hole parameters
(a_{k},M_{k},l)
with the
same initial data
as
\psi
, see (
9.34
). Note that thus
\psi_{(k)}
are future integrable by Lemma
9.2
, and moreover
\psi_{(k)}
are fixed
m
-solutions for any
k
(which follows from the fact that the Killing vector field
\partial_{\varphi}
does not depend on the black hole parameters
(a_{k},M_{k},l)
.)

By using Proposition
9.1
for future integrable solutions we obtain

(9.41)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}.\end{aligned}

Now, by using an
N
energy based energy estimate and the result of Proposition
9.1
for a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}>0
we follow the exact steps of
[
14
]
and obtain that

(9.42)

\sup_{\tau\geq 0}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{i_{t}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}\leq\textBeta\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{i_{t}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}.

Finally, we obtain

(9.43)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{i_{t}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\not{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{i_{t}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi_{(k)}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2},

where the first equality follows from well-posedness of the Klein–Gordon equation and the smooth dependence of
g_{a,M,l}
on
(a,M)
and the fast that
\psi_{(k)}
and
\psi
have the same initial data. The last inequality follows from Fatou’s lemma. It follows from (
9.42
) that
\psi
satisfies (
9.24
). Since
\psi
was arbitrary it follows that
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
. Thus
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
is closed in the topology of
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
, where recall from Proposition
9.3
that
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
is an open subset of
\mathcal{B}_{l}
.

To prove the openess
of
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
in the
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
topology we proceed as follows. We first note that
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
is an open subset of
\mathcal{B}_{l}
, see Proposition
9.3
. We follow similar arguments that proved an analogous result in [
[
17
]
, Section 11, Proposition 11.2.1]. Namely, we want to prove that for

(9.44)

(\mathring{a},\mathring{M})\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}

there exists an
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small and for any rotation parameter
a
such that

(9.45)

|a-\mathring{a}|+|M-\mathring{M}|\leq\epsilon

we also obtain
(a,M)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
. One of the ‘key’ ingredients to be able to repeat the arguments of
[
17
]
in the context of the present paper is the existence of the timelike vector field

(9.46)

W_{\textit{mod}}

see Lemma
9.1
, which is Killing in the region where trapping happens for the fixed azimuthal frequency
m
, see Proposition
9.1
and that that the hypersurface leaves
\{t^{\star}=c\}
are independent of the black hole parameters
a,M,l
, see Remarks,
2.1
,
2.2
.

First, by using the divergence theorem with multiplier
W_{\textit{mod}}
and the fixed frequency Morawetz estimate of Proposition
9.1
(for the inhomogeneous Klein–Gordon with inhomogeneity
F
) we obtain the following

(9.47)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(0,\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}+(Z^{\star}\psi)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(j,m)\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[r_{+},r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}})}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}]}\right)|\partial_{\varphi}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(0,\tau_{2})}|\partial_{t}\psi\cdot F|+|\partial_{\varphi}\psi\cdot F|+|F|^{2}

where

(9.48)

\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\begin{cases}1,\qquad\{r_{+}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}\cup\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}\}\\
0,\qquad\{r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\}.\end{cases}

Now, suppose that
\delta_{0}>0
is sufficiently small and define an interpolating metric

(9.49)

\tilde{g}_{\tau_{2}}=\chi_{\tau_{2}}g_{\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l}+(1-\chi_{\tau_{2}})g_{a,M,l}

where
\chi_{\tau_{2}}=0
in the past of
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}-\delta_{0}\}
and
\chi=1
in the future of
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}
, where moreover
\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\chi_{\tau_{2}}\equiv 0
. We define
\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}
to be the intepolating solution of the wave equation

(9.50)

\Box_{\tilde{g}_{\tau_{2}}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}=0

with
the same initial data
as
\psi
. We note that
\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}
is a Killing vector field for
\tilde{g}_{\tau_{2}}
and therefore the intepolating solution will be supported on the same azimuthal frequency
m
as the original solution
\psi
. Hence, since
(\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l)\in\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
and by (
9.35
) we obtain that
\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}
is future integrable with respect to
(\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l)
. We write

(9.51)

\Box_{g_{\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}=\left(\Box_{g_{\mathring{a},\mathring{M},l}}-\Box_{\tilde{g}_{\tau_{2}}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}\penalty\ \dot{=}\penalty\ F

and approximate as follows

(9.52)

|\left(\Box_{g_{\mathring{a},\mathring{M},\mathring{l}}}-\Box_{\tilde{g}_{\tau_{2}}}\right)\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}\right|^{2}

by using the smooth dependence of
g_{a,M,l}
on
a,M
, where for
\epsilon>0
see (
9.45
). The angular derivative
\not{\nabla}
is with respect to the metric
g_{\mathring{a},\mathring{M}}
.

Keeping in mind that (
9.51
) is supported in the past of
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}
, then we use the integrated estimate (
9.47
) to obtain

(9.53)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[r_{+},r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}})}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}]}\right)\left(J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\partial_{\varphi}\psi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B(m)\epsilon^{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}\right|^{2}d\tau^{\prime}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}-\delta_{0}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq 2}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\tilde{\psi}_{\tau_{2}}\right|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+B(m)\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where we also used finite in time energy estimates, see Lemma
8.1
, and an easy domain of dependence argument.

Now, we note the integrated estimate (
9.12
) of Proposition
9.1
(the fixed azimuthal frequency Morawetz and boundedness estimate) for
j\geq 3

(9.54)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int\int_{D(0,\tau_{2})}\tilde{\zeta}_{\textit{trap}}(r)\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j-1}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}+1}\psi|^{2}+|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(m,j)\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}|\mbox{$\nabla\mkern-13.0mu/$\penalty\hskip 0.70007pt}^{i_{1}}(\partial_{t^{\star}})^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+B(m,j)\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau\left(\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[r_{+},r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}})}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+2\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\bar{r}_{+}]}\right)|\partial_{\varphi}\psi|^{2}.

We use the bound (
9.53
), to absorb the bulk terms on the RHS of (
9.54
) in the LHS of (
9.54
), for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. We obtain that for solutions supported in the fixed azimuthal frequency
|m|>0
the following holds

(9.55)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[0,\tau_{2}]}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau^{\prime}\}}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B(j,m)\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}\sum_{0\leq i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq j}\left|\not{\nabla}^{i_{1}}\partial_{t}^{i_{2}}(Z^{\star})^{i_{3}}\psi\right|^{2}<\infty,\qquad\forall j\geq 3

for all
1\leq\tau_{2}
. In view of (
9.35
) we conclude that
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
is open.

We have concluded that the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG},m}
is non empty and clopen in the
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
topology and therefore (
9.36
) holds.

Therefore, we conclude that the solution
\psi=\psi_{m}
is future integrable. We conclude the proof of the Proposition.
∎

9.5.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

We are ready to prove our main result of the present Section.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

Note the azimuthal mode expansion

(9.56)

\psi=\sum_{m}\psi_{m},

where two distinct azimuthal modes
\psi_{m}
are orthogonal in the
l^{2}
sense.

Then, for
\psi
as in the assumptions of Theorem
9.1
we note from Proposition
9.2
that each
\psi_{m}
is future integrable. Therefore it follows from Corollary
8.2
that the energy estimates (
7.3
), (
7.12
) hold for
\psi_{m}
. Orthogonality of two distinct
\psi_{m}
immediately implies that (
7.3
), (
7.12
) hold for
\psi
.

Thus,
\psi
is sufficiently integrable, as desired.
∎

10.
Proof of the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
1

Now we are ready to prove the Morawetz estimate (
7.3
) of Theorem
1
. We note that we have already proved Corollary
8.2
and Theorem
9.1
which will be used in the rest of the present Section.

We have the following

Proof of the Morawetz estimate of Theorem
1
.

We fix
\tau_{1}>0
. Let
\psi
be a solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) that arises from smooth initial data on
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. Then, in view of Theorem
9.1
, the result of Corollary
8.2
holds for
\psi
.

To conclude the Morawetz estimate (
7.3
) of Theorem
1
we need to prove that the following holds in addition

(10.1)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\sum_{m}\left(1_{|m|>0}|\mathcal{F}_{m}\psi|^{2}\right)+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle(Z^{\star}\psi)^{2}+(\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi\psi])^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi\psi]|^{2}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu},

where for the operator
\mathcal{P}_{trap}
see (
6.42
).

It suffices to prove the above after assuming the following:

(1)

First, we make the assumption that the solution
\psi
is generated from smooth initial data on the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. This assumption is needed to appeal to Theorem
9.1
. It suffices to have this assumption in view of general density arguments.

(2)

Second, for the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we make the assumption that the mean associated with the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}
, satisfies the following

(10.2)

\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})\penalty\ \dot{=}\penalty\ \frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\psi=0.

It suffices to make this assumption since if
\psi
is a solution of the wave equation, see (
1.7
) with
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, then
\psi+c
is also a solution, where
c
is any constant.

We use the main result of the continuity argument Section
9
, see Theorem
9.1
, and the result of Corollary
8.2
to obtain that the following energy estimate holds

(10.3)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\sum_{m}\left(1_{|m|>0}|\mathcal{F}_{m}\psi|^{2}\right)+\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}[\chi_{\tau_{1}}\psi]|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

for all
2\leq 1+\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}\leq\infty
, where for the operator
\mathcal{P}_{\textit{trap}}
see (
6.42
). When
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
we note that the RHS of (
10.3
) is equivalent to
\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}
with a constant
that blows up in the limit
\mu^{2}_{KG}\rightarrow 0
. For the wave equation case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we use the assumption (
10.2
) and use the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality of Lemma
4.3
to obtain (
10.1
).

We have concluded (
11.1
).
∎

11.
Proof of the boundedness estimates of Theorem
1

In this Section we prove the boundedness estimates (
7.4
), (
7.5
) of Theorem
1
.

The proof of the boundedness estimates of Theorem
1
.

We fix
\tau_{1}>0
. Let
\psi
be a solution of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) that arises from smooth initial data on
\{t^{\star}=0\}
. It suffices to have this assumption in view of general density arguments. Then, the results of Corollary
8.2
and of Theorem
9.1
hold for
\psi
.

To conclude the boundedness estimates (
7.4
), (
7.5
) of Theorem
1
we need to prove that the following hold in addition

(11.1)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

(11.2)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

(11.3)

\left(\int_{\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}+\int_{\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cap D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\right)J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}.

Again, it suffices to prove the above assuming the following:

(1)

First, in the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we make the assumption that the mean associated with the hypersurface
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}
, satisfies the following

(11.4)

\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})\penalty\ \dot{=}\penalty\ \frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\psi=0.

It suffices to make this assumption since if
\psi
is a solution of the wave equation, see (
1.7
) with
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, then
\psi+c
is also a solution, where
c
is any constant.

(2)

Second, we make the assumption that
\psi_{0}\equiv 0
, where
\psi=\psi_{0}+\sum_{|m|\neq 0}\psi_{m}
, and
\psi_{m}
is the
m
-th azimuthal mode. It suffices to make this assumption since in view of Proposition
5.3
it is easy to obtain the desired boundedness estimates (
11.1
), (
11.2
), (
11.3
) for axisymmetric solutions. Moreover, this assumption will allow us to bound zeroth order bulk terms from data, see already (
11.6
).

Extending the solution

For reasons that will become apparent in what follows we extend the solution
\psi
from
\{t^{\star}\geq 0\}
to a solution of (
1.7
), that we will again denote by
\psi
, on
\mathcal{M}\cup\mathcal{H}^{-}\cup\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{-}\cup\mathcal{B}
, where
\mathcal{H}^{-},\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{-},\mathcal{B}
are respectively the past event horizon, the past cosmological horizon and the bifurcation spheres. We note that by a domain of dependence argument and by finite in time energy estimates we can extend the solution in such a way so that it satisfies the following two properties:

First, the extended solution satisfies

(11.5)

\int_{\hat{\Sigma}_{0}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=0\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

where
\hat{\Sigma}_{0}
is the image of
\{t^{\star}=0\}
under the Boyer–Lindquist coordinate defined map
t\mapsto-t
.

Second, our extension of
\psi
satisfies inequality (
11.5
) for any spacelike hypersurface in the set
J^{+}(\hat{\Sigma}_{0})\cup J^{-}(\{t^{\star}=0\})
, in the place of
\hat{\Sigma}_{0}
.

Integrated local energy decay for the extended solution

We note that the Boyer–Lindquist defined map
t\mapsto-t
and
a\mapsto-a
is an isometry. Therefore, the result of Corollary
8.2
holds if one goes to the past instead of the future, also see Proposition
7.1
for the reflection symmetry of the set
\mathcal{MS}_{l,\mu_{KG}}
with respect to the map
a\mapsto-a
. Namely we replace
\{t^{\star}=\tau\}
with
\hat{\Sigma}_{\tau}
and by keeping in mind (
11.5
) we obtain

(11.6)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\tau\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}

\displaystyle|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+\zeta_{trap}(r)\left(|\partial_{t}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

where for
\zeta_{trap}
see Remark
7.5
. Note that the derivatives
\partial_{r^{\star}},\partial_{t},\not{\nabla}
are regular on
\mathcal{M}\cup\mathcal{H}^{-}\cup\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{-}\cup\mathcal{B}
. Although inequality (
11.6
) is useful, we need a slightly different integrated energy decay estimate with a more detailed degeneration at trapping, see already (
11.14
). Again, note that we include the zeroth order term in the bulk on the RHS of (
11.6
) in view of the third assumption above and of Theorem
8.3
.

We choose
A_{0},A_{1}>0
and
\delta>0
such that

(11.7)

r_{+}<A_{0}<A_{0}+\delta<A_{0}+2\delta<r_{+}+\epsilon_{red}\qquad\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{red}<A_{1}-2\delta<A_{1}-\delta<A_{1}<\bar{r}_{+},

where for
\epsilon_{red}>0
see the redshift Proposition
5.1
. Now, we smoothly cut-off the solution
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation as follows

(11.8)

\tilde{\psi}=\psi\cdot\chi_{[A_{0},A_{1}]}

where

(11.9)

\chi_{[A_{0},A_{1}]}=\begin{cases}1,\penalty\ r\in[A_{0}+\delta,A_{1}-\delta]\\
0,\penalty\ r\not\in[A_{0},A_{1}].\end{cases}

where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small. We note that

(11.10)

\Box\tilde{\psi}-\mu^{2}_{KG}\tilde{\psi}=\tilde{F}:=\Box\chi_{[A_{0},A_{1}]}\psi+2\nabla\chi\cdot\nabla\psi,

where it is easy to see that

(11.11)

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}|\tilde{F}|^{2}\leq B\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left(|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}\right)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

where in the last inequality we used the integrated estimate (
11.6
).

We now apply Carter’s separation of variables, see Proposition
6.2
, to equation (
11.10
) to obtain

(11.12)

\tilde{u}^{\prime\prime}+(\omega^{2}-V)\tilde{u}=\tilde{H}

where
\tilde{u}
is the frequency localization of
\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{\psi}
, and the support of
\tilde{\psi}
is inherited by
\tilde{u}
. Specifically, we note that
|\tilde{u}|^{2}(-\infty)=0
. Moreover, the RHS of all frequency localized multipliers, see Definition
14.1
are
\mathcal{O}(\tilde{H})
, and therefore are supported in
[A_{0},A_{0}+\delta]\cup[A_{1}-\delta,A_{1}]
. Therefore, we apply ode estimates of Section
16
to conclude the following

(11.13)

\displaystyle b\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left(|\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{trap}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{trap}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap\left([A_{0},A_{0}+\delta]\cup[A_{1}-\delta,A_{1}]\right)}\left(|\psi|^{2}+J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\right),

where for the operator
\mathcal{P}_{trap}
see (
6.42
).

Now, for the wave equation case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
we use (
11.6
) and the assumption (
11.4
) on the vanishing of the mean to obtain

(11.14)

b\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}}\left(|\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}_{trap}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\mathcal{P}_{trap}\tilde{\psi}|^{2}\right)\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}.

A decomposition

In what follows we will use the integrated estimate (
11.14
) to prove the desired boundedness estimates. In short, we will decompose
\tilde{\psi}
into pieces
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
that experience trapping in sufficiently small regions of
r
.

First, we need the following definition

Definition 11.1
.

Let
\epsilon_{part}>0
be sufficiently small and

(11.15)

k=k(\epsilon_{part})=[\epsilon_{part}^{-1}(R^{+}-R^{-})]

where for
R^{\pm}
see Theorem
8.3
.

We denote

(11.16)

a_{1}=R^{-},\qquad b_{k}=R^{+},

and we define the intervals
P_{i}=[a_{i},b_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,k
where
P_{i}\cap P_{j}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
to be such that

(11.17)

\bigcup_{i=1}^{k(\epsilon_{part})}P_{i}=[R^{-},R^{+})

where

(11.18)

|P_{i}|=\epsilon_{part}

for all
i=1,\dots,k
.

Finally, we define

(11.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{0}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\tilde{\lambda}):r_{trap}(\omega,m,\tilde{\lambda})=0\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{i}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\ell):r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in P_{i}\}

for all
i=1,\dots k
, where for
r_{trap}
see Theorem
16.1
.

Remark 11.1
.

In view of the definition of
r_{trap}
, see Theorem
16.1
, we note that each
(\omega,m,\ell)
lies in exactly one
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.

We take
\epsilon_{part}>0
of Definition
11.1
sufficiently small. We can easily construct
\varphi_{\tau}
invariant timelike vector fields

(11.20)

V_{i}

with
i=1,\dots k
, which are Killing in the regions
[a_{i}-\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}},b_{i}+\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}}]
,
i=1,\dots,k,
for a sufficiently small
\epsilon_{part}>0
, where recall that
P_{i}=[a_{i},b_{i})
,
b_{i}-a_{i}=\epsilon_{part}
and
r_{+}<a_{i}<b_{i}<\bar{r}_{+}
for all
i
.

We note that

(11.21)

\tilde{\psi}=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\tilde{\psi}_{i},

for

(11.22)

\tilde{\psi}_{i}\penalty\ \dot{=}\penalty\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\sum_{m\ell}1_{\mathcal{C}_{i}}e^{-i\omega t}e^{im\varphi}S^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\tilde{\psi}^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}d\omega

where for the number
k=k(\epsilon_{part})
and for
\mathcal{C}_{i}
see Definition
11.1
. Note that the equality (
11.22
) is to be understood in an
\textEta^{1}
sense if
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
and in an
\dot{H}^{1}
sense if
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
. Furthermore, we have that

(11.23)

\Box\tilde{\psi}_{i}-\mu^{2}_{KG}\tilde{\psi}_{i}=\tilde{F}_{i},

where
\tilde{F}_{i}=\Box\chi_{[A_{0},A_{1}]}\cdot\tilde{\psi}_{i}+2\nabla\chi_{[A_{0},A_{1}]}\cdot\nabla\tilde{\psi}_{i}
.

The following lemma is straightforward to prove with a pigeonhole principle argument

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\epsilon>0
be sufficiently small. Let
\Psi
be sufficiently integrable, see Definition
6.1
, and let
\Psi\equiv 0
in
\{r\leq r_{+}+\epsilon\}\cup\{r\geq\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon\}
.

Then, there exists a constant
C=C(\Psi)>0
and a dyadic sequence
\{\tau_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
such that
\tau_{n}\rightarrow-\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and

(11.24)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\Psi]n^{\mu}\leq\frac{C}{\tau_{n}}.

Now, we note that
\tilde{\psi}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
are sufficiently integrable, see Definition
6.1
. Since
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
is sufficiently integrable and supported in
\{A_{0}\leq r\leq A_{1}\}
for any
i=0,\dots,k
we note from Lemma
11.1
that for any
i=0,\dots,k
there exists a constant
C_{i}
and a dyadic sequence
\tau_{n}^{(i)}
such that for
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
as in (
11.21
) we obtain

(11.25)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}^{(i)}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\tilde{\psi}_{i}]n^{\mu}\leq\frac{C_{i}}{|\tau_{n}^{(i)}|},

where
\tau_{n}^{(i)}\rightarrow-\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

We apply the energy identity asssociated with the vector field
V_{i}
and
\tilde{\psi}_{i}
between the hypersurfaces
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}^{(i)}\}
,
\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}
. By recalling that the vector field
V_{i}
is Killing in the region
[a_{i}-\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}},b_{i}+\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}}]
, and that in the region
[a_{i}-\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}},b_{i}+\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}}]^{c}
and for the frequencies
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
we have
|r-r_{trap}|\geq b(\epsilon_{part})
, see also the definition (
6.42
) of
\mathcal{P}_{trap}
and the decomposition (
11.22
), we obtain the following

(11.26)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{V_{i}}_{\mu}[\tilde{\psi_{i}}]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\tau_{n}^{(i)}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\{t^{\star}=s\}\cap[a_{i}-\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}},b_{i}+\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}}]^{c}}J^{V_{i}}_{\mu}[\tilde{\psi}_{i}]n^{\mu}+\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{n}^{(i)}\}}J^{V_{i}}_{\mu}[\tilde{\psi}_{i}]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau\}\cap[a_{i}-\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}},b_{i}+\frac{\epsilon_{part}}{10^{4}}]^{c}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\mathcal{P}_{trap}\tilde{\psi}_{i}]n^{\mu}+\frac{BC_{i}}{\tau_{n}^{(i)}}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+\frac{BC_{i}}{|\tau_{n}^{(i)}|}

for
\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
, where we also used (
11.25
) and the already proven integrated decay estimate (
11.14
). Now, by taking
n\rightarrow\infty
we conclude that

(11.27)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}[\tilde{\psi}]n^{\mu}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

after summing over
i=0,\dots,k
. Therefore, we have proved the boundedness for
\psi
away from the horizons
\mathcal{H}^{+},\bar{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
.

Now, it is an easy application of the redshift estimate, see Proposition
5.2
and of the already proven Morawetz estimate (
10.1
) to prove that

(11.28)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}\cap[r_{+},A_{0}+\delta]}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+B\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}ds\int_{\{t^{\star}=s\}\cap[A_{0}+\delta,A_{0}+2\delta]}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu},

in view of the fact that
J^{N}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\sim J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}
in the region
\{r_{+}\leq r\leq A_{0}+\delta\}
. We proceed similarly to prove

(11.29)

\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}\cap[A_{1}-\delta,\bar{r}_{+}]}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}.

Therefore, in view of (
11.27
) and (
11.28
), (
11.29
) we conclude (
11.1
).

For the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
we conclude (
11.2
) from (
11.1
). For the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, in view of the assumption (
11.4
) and the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality of Lemma
4.3
, we conclude (
11.2
) from (
11.1
).

Finally, in order to obtain the boundedness of the horizon fluxes (
11.3
) we appeal to the redshift estimate of Proposition
5.2
and the integrated estimate (
11.14
).

∎

12.
The axisymmetric case

We prove the Morawetz and boundedness estimates of Theorem
1
, for axisymmetric solutions of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
). Note that we do not appeal to a continuity argument.

Proof.

First, the boundedness estimates

(12.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}

\displaystyle\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{2}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J^{n}_{\mu}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2}

follow immediately from Proposition
5.3
.

Second, we prove the Morawetz estimate (
10.1
). In the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
it suffices to make the assumption

(12.2)

\underline{\psi}(\tau_{1})\penalty\ \dot{=}\penalty\ \frac{1}{|\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}|}\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}\psi=0.

It suffices to make this assumption since if
\psi
is a solution of the wave equation, see (
1.7
) with
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, then
\psi+c
is also a solution, where
c
is any constant.

Now, in view of Lemma
9.2
and the now established boundedness estimates (
7.4
), (
7.5
) we have that
\psi
is future integrable. Therefore, we can now use the result of Theorem
8.1
and obtain

(12.3)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)\left(|\partial_{t}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu}+|\psi|^{2},

where
\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)=\left(1-\frac{r_{\Delta,\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}.

In the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
we conclude the desired Morawetz estimate from (
12.3
). In the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
, in view of the assumption (
12.2
) and the Poincare–Wirtinger inequality of Lemma
4.3
we obtain

(12.4)

\displaystyle\int\int_{D(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\psi|^{2}+|\partial_{r^{\star}}\psi|^{2}+|Z^{\star}\psi|^{2}+\zeta_{\textit{trap}}(r)\left(|\partial_{t}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}\psi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\{t^{\star}=\tau_{1}\}}J_{\mu}^{n}[\psi]n^{\mu},

and we conclude the desired Morawetz estimate as well.

Now, in view of the Morawetz estimate (
12.4
), the redshift estimate of Proposition
5.2
and the estimates (
12.1
) we obtain the boundedness estimates (
7.4
), (
7.5
).
∎

13.
The critical points of the potential
V_{0}

The rest of this paper is dedicated to the formulation and proof of Theorem
8.3
, of Section
8
. We will need the present preliminary Section as well as Sections
14
,
15
as well. Theorem
8.3
will then be proven in Section
16
as a Corollary of Theorem
16.1
.

The main result of the present Section is Lemma
13.1
. Specifically, we study the properties of the potential
V_{0}
, see (
6.27
), for fixed frequencies
(\omega,m,\ell)
, and prove that
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}
attains at most one critical point in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
.

Lemma 13.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Then, the function

(13.1)

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr},

where
V_{0}
is the potential from equation (
6.27
), attains at most one critical point in the interval
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
, which is moreover a local maximum. Furthermore, if the local maximum exists, then we also have

(13.2)

\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}\right)(r_{V_{0},max})<0.

Finally, the potential

(13.3)

V_{0}

attains at most two critical points in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
that satisfy

(13.4)

r_{+}\leq r_{V_{0},\textit{min}}<r_{V_{0},\textit{max}}\leq\bar{r}_{+},

which are respectively a local minimum and a local maximum.

Proof.

First, note the following

(13.5)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}\right)=-6\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)r^{2}+12M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)r-2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}+(2am\Xi)^{2}+\frac{4a^{5}m\Xi\omega}{l^{2}}.

The polynomial of equation (
13.5
) enjoys the same roots as the polynomial

(13.6)

r^{2}-\frac{2M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}r+\frac{2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}\Xi-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\Xi\omega}{l^{2}}}{6\Xi(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})}.

The roots of the polynomial (
13.6
) are

(13.7)

r_{0,\pm}=\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})}\pm\sqrt{\left(\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})}\right)^{2}-\frac{2a^{2}\Xi\tilde{\lambda}-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\Xi\omega}{l^{2}}}{6\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}}.

Recall from Lemma
6.1
that the following hold

(13.8)

\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi>0,\qquad\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}>0.

For the case
am\omega\geq 0
we obtain that since
\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}<\Xi
then we have

(13.9)

\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}>\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\implies\frac{\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}}<1,

which implies

(13.10)

\real(r_{0,-})<\frac{M}{\Xi}<M<r_{+},

which concludes that (
13.1
) attains at most one critical point for the case
am\omega\geq 0
since then the only potential root in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
is
r_{0,+}
.

For the case
am\omega<0
we proceed as follows. First, we write
r_{0,-}
in the following manner

(13.11)

r_{0,-}=\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}\left(1-\sqrt{1-\frac{\left(2a^{2}\Xi\tilde{\lambda}-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\Xi\omega}{l^{2}}\right)\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}{6M^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)^{2}}}\right).

We have

(13.12)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(2a^{2}\Xi\tilde{\lambda}-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\Xi\omega}{l^{2}}\right)\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}{6M^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)^{2}}=\frac{a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m^{2}\Xi-\frac{2a^{3}m\omega}{l^{2}}\right)\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}{3M^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{2}\Xi^{2}}{3M^{2}}\frac{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m^{2}\Xi-\frac{2a^{3}m\omega}{l^{2}}\right)}{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)}\frac{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)}.

By recalling the bounds

(13.13)

\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}<\frac{1}{4},\quad\left|\frac{a}{M}\right|\leq\frac{12}{10}

from Lemma
3.2
,
and that (
13.8
) hold we obtain

(13.14)

\frac{a^{2}\Xi^{2}}{3M^{2}}\leq\frac{3}{4}

and

(13.15)

\frac{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m^{2}\Xi-\frac{2a^{3}m\omega}{l^{2}}\right)}{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)}\frac{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}{\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)}\leq 1.

Therefore, we bound the right hand side of (
13.12
) as follows

(13.16)

\textit{RHS of }\eqref{eq: /lem proof /sec: Carters separation/ the critical points of V0/ eq 5}\leq\frac{3}{4}.

Now, we note the inequality

(13.17)

\sqrt{1-x}\geq 1-\frac{2}{3}x,\qquad\textit{for}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu0\leq x\leq\frac{3}{4},

where the equality holds for

(13.18)

x=0,\qquad x=\frac{3}{4}.

Νow that we have established the bound (
13.16
), we use inequality (
13.17
) to obtain the following bound

(13.19)

\displaystyle r_{0,-}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}\frac{2}{3}\frac{\frac{2a^{2}\Xi\tilde{\lambda}-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\omega\Xi}{l^{2}}}{6\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}}{\left(\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}\right)^{2}}=\frac{2}{3}\frac{\frac{2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}\Xi-(2am\Xi)^{2}-\frac{4a^{5}m\omega\Xi}{l^{2}}}{6\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}}{\left|\frac{M(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)}{\Xi\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)}\right|}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{9}\frac{a^{2}}{M}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}\Xi-2m^{2}\Xi^{2}-2\frac{a^{3}}{l^{2}}m\omega\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi}

\displaystyle<\frac{2}{9}\frac{a^{2}}{M}\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}-2\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}am\omega\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi}

\displaystyle<\frac{4}{9}\frac{a^{2}}{M^{2}}M<\frac{4}{9}\left(\frac{12}{10}\right)^{2}M<M<r_{+}

where we used that
\frac{2\tilde{\lambda}-2\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}am\omega\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi}<2
, which concludes that (
13.1
) attains at most one critical point for the case
am\omega<0
since the only potential root in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
is
r_{0,+}
.

Now, in view of the already proven

(13.20)

r_{0,-}<r_{+}<r_{0,+}

we conclude that

(13.21)

\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}\right)(r=r_{0,+})<0,

and therefore
r_{0,+}
is a local maximum for the function

(13.22)

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}.

Finally, to conclude that indeed
V_{0}
attains at most two critical points in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
which satisfy

(13.23)

r_{+}\leq r_{V_{0},\textit{min}}<r_{V_{0},\textit{max}}\leq\bar{r}_{+}

we inspect the form of the function

(13.24)

\frac{d}{dr}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}\right),

see (
13.5
), and also use that

(13.25)

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

attains at most one critical point in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. We conclude (
13.2
) from (
13.21
).

We conclude the Lemma.
∎

14.
The currents and the frequency localized multipliers

We use the notation

(14.1)

{}^{\prime}=\frac{d}{dr^{\star}},

see Section
2.5
for the tortoise coordinate.

14.1.
The currents for
u

We define the following

Definition 14.1
.

Let
V
be the potential of Carter’s separation of variables, see Proposition
6.2
. We define the currents

(14.2)

\displaystyle Q^{h}[u]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muh\real(u^{\prime}\bar{u})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}|u|^{2},

\displaystyle Q^{y}[u]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muy(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2}),

\displaystyle Q^{f}[u]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muf(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}-V)|u|^{2})+f^{\prime}\real(u^{\prime}\bar{u})-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}|u|^{2}.

Note that for the last current of (
14.2
) we chose
h=\frac{df}{dr^{\star}}
and
y=f
and summed the first two currents of (
14.2
).

Lemma 14.1
.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
). The derivatives of the currents of Definition
14.1
are

(14.3)

\displaystyle(Q^{h}[u])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=h|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\Big(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\Big)|u|^{2}+h\real(u\bar{H}),

\displaystyle(Q^{y}[u])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\Big(y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\Big)|u|^{2}+2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})

\displaystyle=y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\Big(\omega^{2}y^{\prime}-(yV)^{\prime}\Big)|u|^{2}+2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H}),

\displaystyle(Q^{f}[u])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2f^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)|u|^{2}+\real\left(2f\bar{H}u^{\prime}+f^{\prime}\bar{H}u\right).

14.2.
The currents for
\Psi

We study the ode for
\Psi
instead of the ode for
u=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi
. The currents of the present Section will be used in Section
16.5
.

Lemma 14.2
.

Let
u
be a smooth solution of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
). Then, the function
\Psi
, where
u=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi
, satisfies the following ode

(14.4)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime\prime}+\frac{2r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Psi^{\prime}+\Psi\Big(\omega^{2}-\tilde{V}\Big)=\frac{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}},

where

(14.5)

\tilde{V}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muV_{0}+V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}=\frac{(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2m\omega a\Xi)\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\Delta\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2},

where for
V_{0},V_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}
see (
6.27
).

Proof.

This is straightforward from equation (
6.25
).
∎

We define the following

Definition 14.2
.

The modified currents that correspond to the new form of the equation are

(14.6)

\displaystyle Q^{h}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muh\real(\Psi\bar{\Psi}^{\prime})+\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}h|\Psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}|\Psi|^{2},

\displaystyle Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi]

\displaystyle\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0muy|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+y(\omega^{2}-\tilde{V})|\Psi|^{2},

Lemma 14.3
.

Let
\Psi
be a smooth solution of (
14.4
). The derivatives of the currents of Definition
14.2
are

(14.7)

\displaystyle(Q^{h}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=h|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(\left(\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}h\right)^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\right)|\Psi|^{2}+\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right),

\displaystyle(Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\Big(y^{\prime}-y\frac{4r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Big)|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Big(y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-\tilde{V})-y\tilde{V}^{\prime}\Big)|\Psi|^{2}+\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right).

By adding the two currents, after taking
f=y
and
h=\frac{df}{dr^{\star}}
, we obtain a third current

(14.8)

\displaystyle(Q^{f}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi])^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(2f^{\prime}-\frac{4r\Delta(r)f}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(\left(\frac{r\Delta(r)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}f^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}-f\tilde{V}^{\prime}\right)|\Psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\real\left(\frac{2\Psi f^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)+\real\left(\frac{f\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right).

14.3.
The currents
Q^{\partial_{t}}[u],Q^{K^{+}}[u],Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]

We define the following currents

Definition 14.3
.

We define the currents

(14.9)

\displaystyle Q^{\partial_{t}}[u]=\omega\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u}),\qquad Q^{K^{+}}[u]=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u}),\qquad Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u}).

Lemma 14.4
.

Let
u
be a smooth solutions of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
). The derivatives of the currents of Definition
14.3
are respectively

(14.10)

\displaystyle\left(Q^{\partial_{t}}[u]\right)^{\prime}=\omega\imaginary(H\bar{u}),\qquad\left(Q^{K^{+}}[u]\right)^{\prime}=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(H\bar{u}),\qquad\left(Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]\right)^{\prime}=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(H\bar{u}).

15.
The frequency regimes

15.1.
The de Sitter frequencies

We define the ‘de Sitter frequencies’ as follows

(15.1)

\mathcal{DSF}=\{(\omega,m):\penalty\ |\omega|\in\left[0,\frac{|am|\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right]\}

and note that they are disjoint from the set of superradiant frequencies, see Definition
6.7
.

For high angular frequencies, we define the ‘high de Sitter frequency regime’
\mathcal{F}_{dS}
, see the next Section
15.2
, which note that, for certain black hole parameters will correspond to a trapped frequency regime, also see Proposition
16.1
.

Note that the interior of the set (
15.1
) is not empty, in contrast with the Kerr case where
\bar{r}_{+}=\infty
, see Lemma
3.2
.

15.2.
The frequency regimes

To ease the comparison with
[
17
]
, we retain the notation for the frequency regimes introduced there, while note that we here introduce the frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}
.

Definition 15.1

(Definition of the frequency regimes)
.

Let

(15.2)

\omega_{\textit{low}},\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}},\qquad\lambda_{\textit{low}},\qquad\alpha>0

be sufficiently small, sufficiently large, sufficiently small and sufficiently small positive real parameters respectively. We choose these parameters later, see already Section
16.9
.

Then, we cover the frequency space
\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}\right)
by the following (potentially overlapping) frequency regimes:

(15.3)

\displaystyle\text{The high de Sitter frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\tilde{\lambda}\geq\omega_{\textit{high}}\}\cap\{(\omega,m)\in\mathcal{DSF},\penalty\ am\omega\geq 0\},

\displaystyle\text{which will be further split into a trapped and non-trapped case.}

\displaystyle\text{The enlarged high superradiant frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\tilde{\lambda}\geq\left(\frac{|a|\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha\right)^{-1}\omega_{\textit{high}}\}\cap\{am\omega\in\Big(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\Big)\}

\displaystyle\text{The bounded frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\flat}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{high}},|\tilde{\lambda}|<\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\omega^{2}_{\textit{high}}\}

\displaystyle\text{The $\lambda$--dominated frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\tilde{\lambda}\geq\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\omega^{2}_{\textit{high}},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\tilde{\lambda}>\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}(\omega^{2}+a^{2}m^{2})\}\cap\{am\omega\not{\in}\Big[0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\Big)\}

\displaystyle\text{The high\penalty\ $\omega\sim\lambda$ frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\natural}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{high}},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\lambda_{\textit{low}}\tilde{\lambda}\leq\omega^{2}+a^{2}m^{2}\leq\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\tilde{\lambda}\}\cap\{am\omega\not{\in}\Big[0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\Big)\}

\displaystyle\text{which will be further split into a trapped and non-trapped case.}

\displaystyle\text{The $\omega$\penalty\ dominated frequency regime:}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{high}},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu|\tilde{\lambda}|<\lambda_{\textit{low}}(\omega^{2}+a^{2}m^{2})\}\cap\{am\omega\not{\in}\Big[0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha|\tilde{\lambda|}\Big)\}.

By inspecting (
15.3
) we have that

(15.4)

\mathcal{F}_{dS}\cup\mathcal{F}^{\natural}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cup\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}=\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}\right).

Remark 15.1
.

Note that in the
\Lambda=0
Kerr case we obtain
\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}=\emptyset
, see
[
17
]
, since
\bar{r}_{+}(\Lambda=0)=\infty
. Moreover, note that in the Schwarzschild–de Sitter case we also obtain
\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}=\emptyset
.

For certain black hole parameters the frequency regimes
\mathcal{F}_{dS}
admits trapping, see already Section
16.3
, even for frequencies
\omega
sufficiently close to
0
. This corresponds to the existence of
\partial_{t}
orthogonal trapped null geodesics in the background manifold
\mathcal{M}
of Kerr–de Sitter, see Section
B
.

Note the following lemma

Remark 15.2
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Let the parameters
\lambda_{\textit{low}}(a,M,l),\penalty\ \omega_{\textit{high}}(a,M,l),\penalty\ \alpha(a,M,l)>0
be arbitrary positive real numbers. Then, the only frequency regime intersections that are not empty are

(15.5)

\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{F}^{\sharp},\qquad\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}.

To prove this we note that for
|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{high}}
and
am\omega\in\left(0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\right)
, we obtain

(15.6)

\displaystyle 0<am\omega<\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\leq\left(\frac{a^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\right)\tilde{\lambda}\implies|a\omega_{\textit{high}}|\leq\left(\frac{a^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\right)\tilde{\lambda}

where we used that for
am\omega\geq 0
we have
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2}
, from Lemma
6.1
, and that
|m|\geq 1
. We readily obtain

(15.7)

\tilde{\lambda}\geq\left(\frac{|a|\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha\right)^{-1}\omega_{\textit{high}}.

We conclude that the only frequency regimes interesections that are not empty are
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{F}^{\sharp},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}
. Now, we have that (
15.7
) implies that
\tilde{\lambda}\geq\omega_{high}
in view of the fact that
\frac{a}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}<1,\alpha<1
.

16.
Fixed frequency analysis: Proof of Theorem
8.3

The main result of this Section is Theorem
16.1
. Theorem
8.3
is a direct Corollary.

16.1.
Notation

For convenience we use the notation on the primed derivatives
{}^{\prime}=\frac{d}{dr^{\star}}
and on the constants
b,B
already discussed in the beginning of Section
8
.

Throughout this Section we use the notation introduced in Definition
6.4
, namely that

(16.1)

\tilde{\lambda}=\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}.

16.2.
The fixed frequency Theorem
16.1

We have the following fixed frequency ode result

Theorem 16.1
.

Let
l>0

(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Then, there exist parameters

(16.2)

E>0,\qquad\alpha^{-1}>0,\qquad(\lambda_{\textit{low}})^{-1}>0,\qquad(\omega_{\textit{low}})^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,

\displaystyle r_{+}<R^{-}<R^{+}<\bar{r}_{+},

and an

(16.3)

r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in(R^{-},R^{+})\cup\{0\}

such that for all
r_{-\infty}
sufficiently close to
r_{+}
and all
r_{+\infty}
sufficiently close to
\bar{r}_{+}
and for any sufficiently large
\mathcal{C}>0
there exists a constant
B(\mathcal{C},r^{\star}_{\pm\infty})
, as in Section
16.1
, such that for smooth solutions

(16.4)

u(\omega,m,\ell,r)

of Carter’s inhomogeneous radial ode, see (
6.25
), where recall the rescaling
u=\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}\Psi
, that moreover satisfy the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have the following integrated estimate

(16.5)

\displaystyle 1_{\{\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{m=0\}\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda}+\mu_{KG}^{2})|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}+1_{\{|m|>0\}}\cdot\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{\infty}}\left(|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+1_{(\mathcal{F}_{\flat})^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}+\omega^{2}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\cdot 1_{\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{m=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\big|\omega\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\Big|+\Big|\omega\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\big|+|H|^{2}+|\Psi^{\prime}H|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B1_{|m|>0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\real(u\bar{H})\right|+\left|\real(u\bar{H})\right|+\left|\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\cdot 1_{\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cup\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right||\imaginary(\bar{u}H)|+\left|\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right||\imaginary(\bar{u}H)|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|\omega-\omega_{+}m||\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m||u|^{2}(-\infty),

where for the definition of the frequency regimes
\mathcal{F}^{\sharp},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{\flat},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{dS},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{\sharp},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}
see Section
15
, and for
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.48
).

We recall from (
15.4
) that we cover the entire frequency space

(16.6)

\mathcal{F}_{dS}\cup\mathcal{F}^{\natural}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cup\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}=\mathbb{R}\times\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\{m\}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\geq|m|}

The parameters
\omega_{high},\omega_{low}^{-1},\lambda_{low}^{-1},E,\alpha
will be constrained to some largeness, where the largeness restriction only depends on the black hole parameters
a,M,l
. Until the parameters are fixed then the constants
b,B
, discussed previously, will depend on these parameters. We will fix the parameters at the end of the present Section, see already Section
16.9
.

16.3.
Multipliers for the high de Sitter frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}

This frequency regime is non existent in the
\Lambda=0
Kerr case, since
\bar{r}_{+}(\Lambda=0)=\infty
see Lemma
3.2
.

This frequency regime is non-superradiant and when

(16.7)

\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\leq 1,\quad|a|\not{=}0

holds, then it is subject to trapping. This is related to the presence of trapped null geodesics orthogonal to
\partial_{t}
, see Section
B
.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. For any

(16.8)

\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,\qquad E>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}(\omega_{high})
there exist smooth multipliers
f,\penalty\ h,\penalty\ y
, satisfying the uniform bounds

(16.9)

|f|+|f^{\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime\prime}|+|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime\prime}|+|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B,

and there exists an

(16.10)

r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in(r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away})\cup\{0\},

where
\epsilon_{away}(a,M,l)>0
, such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.11)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}(1+\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}\leq B

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|f\real(u\bar{H})|+|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|h\real(u\bar{H})|+|f\real(u\bar{H})|+|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)|dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)dr^{\star}.

Proof of Proposition
16.1
.

We have that

(16.12)

V_{0}-\omega^{2}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}

and we compute

(16.13)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}=\frac{d}{dr}\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)-\frac{4am\Xi r\left(\omega\left(a^{2}+r^{2}\right)-am\Xi\right)}{\left(a^{2}+r^{2}\right)^{3}},

which we also write as

(16.14)

\displaystyle\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}\Big(r^{3}\left(4a^{3}m\Xi\omega-2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}-2\tilde{\lambda}l^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+r^{2}\left(6\tilde{\lambda}l^{2}M-12al^{2}mM\Xi\omega\right)

\displaystyle+r\left(4a^{5}m\Xi\omega-2a^{4}\tilde{\lambda}-2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}l^{2}+4a^{2}l^{2}m^{2}\Xi^{2}\right)+4a^{3}l^{2}mM\Xi\omega-2a^{2}\tilde{\lambda}l^{2}M\Big).

First, in view of Lemma

we have that

\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2}-2\frac{a^{2}\Xi^{2}}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}m^{2}\geq\left(1-2\frac{a^{2}}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\Xi^{2}m^{2}\geq bm^{2}.

for any
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{dS}
. A similar argument also shows that

\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi=\epsilon_{2}\tilde{\lambda}+(1-\epsilon_{2})\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\geq\epsilon_{2}\tilde{\lambda}+\left(1-\epsilon_{2}-2\frac{a^{2}}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\Xi^{2}m^{2}\geq b(\epsilon_{2})\tilde{\lambda},

for some sufficiently small
\epsilon_{2}(a,M,l)>0
which we now fix.

Since

(16.17)

we have that for

the following holds

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}.

for any sufficiently large
\omega_{high}>0
.

Now, let
\epsilon_{trap}>0
be sufficiently small and let
\omega_{high}>0
be sufficiently large. We study the two cases

\displaystyle\text{(Trap)}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{dS}:\penalty\ |\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}-\omega^{2}|\leq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}\},

\displaystyle\text{(Not Trap)}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{dS}:\penalty\ |\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}-\omega^{2}|>\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}\}.

We study the first case

). For the sake of contradiction, suppose that the maximum of

is attained at the cosmological horizon
\bar{r}_{+}
. Then, we would have that

Then, we use (

) for

sufficiently small and we compute

Therefore, in view of Lemma

).

Now, in view of Lemma

we have that for any

sufficiently small the critical point

(the critical point cannot be attained at

In view of (

), (

) and Lemma

we can now rewrite

see (

for all

in
\mathcal{F}_{dS}
. In view of (

Therefore, for a sufficiently large

and a sufficiently small

We define

For a sufficiently small

\displaystyle\leq Q^{f}(\infty)-Q^{f}(-\infty)+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|+|f\real(u\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|+|f\real(u\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star},

where we also used (

In order to bound the boundary terms at

In view of the non trapping property (Not Trap) we have that

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(r_{V,max}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{10},r_{V,max}+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{10})^{\star}}\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\right)|u|^{2}dr^{\star}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h\real(u\bar{H})dr^{\star}.

) we take

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(r_{V,max}-\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{10},r_{V,max}+\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}}{10})^{\star}}\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\right)|u|^{2}dr^{\star}\leq B|\int_{\mathbb{R}}h\real(u\bar{H})|dr^{\star}.

Then, we sum the above (

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+(1+\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Bf(\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(r^{\star}=\infty)-Bf(-\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(r^{\star}=-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|+|f\real(u\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|h\real(u\bar{H})|+|f\real(u\bar{H})|+2|f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}.

Similarly to the argument presented before, see (

), we integrate the energy identity of Lemma

associated with the current
-EQ^{K^{+}}-EQ^{\bar{K}^{+}}
, for a sufficiently large

, we sum it to the above (

).

\epsilon^{\prime}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0
.

We discuss the second case of (

)
. We note that the maximum of

We discuss the third case of (

)
