∎

We have the following remarks

Remark 16.1
.

We claim that for a sufficiently small
0\leq a\ll M,l
the set
(\text{Trap})
, see (
16.19
), would be empty, for any
\epsilon_{trap}>0
sufficiently small. To prove our claim we note that for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{dS}
we have
0\leq|\omega|\leq\frac{|am|\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\leq\frac{|am|\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}
for any
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. Therefore, for any
0\leq|a|\ll M,l
sufficiently small we have that
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})\geq b\tilde{\lambda}
and
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}
and therefore, in view of Lemma
13.1
, we have that
\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}
, where the constant
b
is independent of
\epsilon_{trap}
. Therefore, for any
\epsilon_{trap}>0
sufficiently small the set
(Trap)
is empty in the slowly rotating case
0\leq|a|\ll M,l
.

Remark 16.2
.

When the condition
\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\leq 1
holds, see Section
B
, then we have that
(Trap)\neq\emptyset
. Specifically, there exists a sequence of frequencies
(\omega_{n},m_{n},\ell_{n})\in\mathcal{F}_{dS}
such that

\omega^{2}_{n}-\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}(r,a,M,l,\omega_{n},m_{n},\ell_{n})\rightarrow 0,\qquad\omega_{n}\rightarrow 0.

Namely, there exists a trapped null geodesic which is orthogonal to
\partial_{t}
.

16.4.
Multipliers for the enlarged high superradiant frequency regime
\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}

This frequency regime is superradiant and ‘quantitatively’ non-trapped, where for the later see already Lemma
16.2
.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.2
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. For any

(16.58)

\alpha^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{high}>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}(\omega_{high},\alpha)
, there exist smooth functions
f,h,\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
, satisfying the uniform bound

(16.59)

|f|+|f^{\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime\prime}|+|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|\chi_{1}|+|\chi_{2}|+|\chi_{1}^{\prime}|+|\chi_{2}^{\prime}|\leq B,

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.60)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right|+\left|(f^{\prime}+h)\real(u\bar{H})\right|+\left|\chi_{2}(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|+\left|\chi_{1}(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

First, note the following lemma

Lemma 16.1
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\omega_{high}>0
. For any sufficiently large

(16.61)

\alpha^{-1}>0

then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}(\alpha,\omega_{high})
we have

(16.62)

\tilde{\lambda}-2a\omega m\Xi\geq b\tilde{\lambda}>0.

Proof.

We use the following bound

(16.63)

-2am\omega\Xi\geq-2\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}-2a\Xi\alpha\tilde{\lambda},

which follows immediately from the definition of the enlarged high superradiant frequency regime
\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}
, see Section
15
, to obtain

(16.64)

\tilde{\lambda}-2am\omega\Xi\geq\tilde{\lambda}-2\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}-2a\Xi\alpha\tilde{\lambda}.

In view of the inequality
r_{+}^{2}>a^{2}
, see Lemma
3.2
, we conclude that for a sufficiently small
0<\epsilon(a,M,l)<1
we obtain

(16.65)

\frac{2a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\leq 1-\epsilon(a,M,l).

Therefore, from (
16.65
), (
16.64
) in conjunction with the inequality
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}\geq\Xi^{2}m^{2}
for
am\omega>0
, see Lemma
6.1
, we obtain

(16.66)

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}-2\frac{(am\Xi)^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{\lambda}+\Xi^{2}m^{2}\left(\epsilon(a,M,l)-1\right)\geq\left(1-\frac{\epsilon(a,M,l)}{2}\right)\tilde{\lambda}+\frac{\epsilon(a,M,l)}{2}\tilde{\lambda}-\Xi^{2}m^{2}\left(1-\epsilon(a,M,l)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{\epsilon(a,M,l)}{2}\right)m^{2}\Xi^{2}-(1-\epsilon(a,M,l))\Xi^{2}m^{2}+\frac{\epsilon(a,M,l)}{2}\tilde{\lambda}

\displaystyle\geq b\tilde{\lambda},

for a sufficiently small
\epsilon(a,M,l)>0
.

Finally, by using equations (
16.64
), (
16.66
), we take
\alpha>0
sufficiently small and conclude.
∎

The following lemma is a quantitative manifestation of the fact that the high (enlarged) superradiant frequencies are not trapped.

Lemma 16.2
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then, for any

(16.67)

\alpha^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}(\alpha,\omega_{high})
there exists a unique global maximum
r_{V,\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
of
V
, where we have

(16.68)

V(r_{V,\textit{max}})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Proof.

We study the following cases

(16.69)

\displaystyle am\omega\in\left[\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\right),\qquad am\omega\in\left(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\mskip 3.0mu\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right).

In this proof, we use

(16.70)

r_{V_{0},\textit{max}},\qquad r_{V,\textit{max}}

to denote respectively the locations of the unique global maximums of the potentials
V_{0},V
.

[formula]
we use Lemma
16.1
, in view of the that
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})\geq 0,\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}
and conclude that if we take
\alpha>0
sufficiently small, then

(16.71)

V_{0}(r_{V_{0},\textit{max}})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda},

for
r_{V_{0},max}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Therefore, by taking
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large we obtain the desired result for
V
.

[formula]
, namely

(16.72)

am\omega\in\left(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)

we proceed as follows. There exists aν
r_{s}(\omega,m)\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
such that

(16.73)

am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}.

We use Lemma
16.1
and obtain
\omega^{2}-V_{0}(r_{s})<-b\tilde{\lambda}
which implies

(16.74)

V_{0}(r_{s})-\omega^{2}>b\tilde{\lambda}.

and therefore we trivially obtain

(16.75)

V_{0}(r_{V_{0},\textit{max}})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Therefore, in all the cases of (
16.69
) we have concluded that for any
\alpha>0
sufficiently small there exist a global maximum
r_{V_{0},\textit{max}}
, of
V_{0}
, such that

(16.76)

V_{0}(r_{V_{0},\textit{max}})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Then, for a sufficiently large
\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
we obtain

(16.77)

V(r_{V,\textit{max}})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda},

where
r_{V,\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
is the value of the global maximum of
V
.

We conclude the proof.
∎

Moreover, note the following lemma.

Lemma 16.3
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then, for any

(16.78)

\alpha^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,\qquad\delta^{-1}>0,

all sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}^{\sharp}(\omega_{high},\alpha)
the following hold

(16.79)

\displaystyle V(r)-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad r\in(r_{V,\textit{max}}-\delta,r_{V,\textit{max}}+\delta)

\displaystyle-(r-r_{V,\textit{max}})\frac{dV}{dr}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}(r-r_{V,\textit{max}})^{2},\qquad r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}].

Proof.

The first inequality of (
16.79
), follows readily from Lemma
16.2
by the continuity of
V
, after taking
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

For the second inequality of equation (
16.79
) we proceed as follows. We compute the derivative

(16.80)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r)=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(\tilde{\lambda}-2a\omega m\Xi)-2\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(2r\frac{am\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right),

and we note that, in view of Lemma
16.1
, the following hold

(16.81)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(r_{+})(\tilde{\lambda}-2a\omega m\Xi)-2\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(2r\frac{am\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}>0,

(16.82)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(\bar{r}_{+})(\tilde{\lambda}-2a\omega m\Xi)-2\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(2r\frac{am\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}<0,

where we used inequality (
16.62
) and that
am\omega\in\left(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\right)
for
\alpha>0
sufficiently small.

We claim that the potential
V_{0}
attains exactly one critical point
r_{V_{0},\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. To prove this note that if the potential
V_{0}
attained more than one critical points, then it would have to attain at least three critical points, because of the behaviour of the derivatives (
16.81
), (
16.82
). But then the functions
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}
and
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}
would have to change sign at least three times. This implies that

(16.83)

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

would attain at least 2 local extrema in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. This is a contradiction to Lemma
13.1
. Therefore, the potential
V_{0}
attains exactly one critical point
r_{V_{0}}\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
.

Again, in view of Lemma
13.1
, and specifically in view of the fact that the function
(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}
is strictly negative, and therefore for
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large the function

(16.84)

(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV}{dr}

is strictly negative. Therefore, we obtain the second inequality of (
16.79
).

Now, we prove the first inequality of (
16.79
) which is a quantitative manifestation of that the enlarged high superradiant frequencies are not trapped. For the superradiant frequencies
\mathcal{SF}
we use that there exists a
r_{s}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
such that
am\omega=\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{s}^{2}+a^{2}}
. We easily conclude that

(16.85)

V(r_{s})-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}

and therefore

(16.86)

\max_{r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})}V(r)-\omega^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Therefore, for
\delta>0
sufficiently small and
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large the inequalities (
16.79
) hold.

Now, we prove the first inequality of (
16.79
) for the enlarged superradiant frequencies

(16.87)

am\omega\in\big(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\big).

Specifically, for
am\omega\in\big(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\big)
we proceed as follows.

We have that

(16.88)

V_{0}(r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha})-\omega^{2}=\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Big|_{r=r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha}}(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{(r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha})^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}

Now, we note that as
\alpha\rightarrow 0
we have that

(16.89)

\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Big|_{r=r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha}}(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)\sim\sqrt{\alpha}(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi),\qquad\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{(r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha})^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\alpha\left(|a|\tilde{\lambda}+(am)^{2}\right)

Therefore, we immediately obtain that

(16.90)

V_{0}(r_{+}+\sqrt{\alpha})-\omega^{2}\geq b(\alpha)\tilde{\lambda}.

We may now easily conclude the desired result.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
16.2
.

Proof of Proposition
16.2
.

Let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small (also see Lemma
16.3
). We define a smooth multiplier function
h
, such that

(16.91)

h=\begin{cases}1,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{V,\textit{max}}-\frac{\delta}{2},r_{V,\textit{max}}+\frac{\delta}{2})\\
0,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{V,\textit{max}}-\delta,r_{V,\textit{max}}+\delta)^{c}.\end{cases}

We define a smooth multiplier function
f
, such that

(16.92)

f=(r-r_{V,\textit{max}})\tilde{f}

where
\tilde{f},\frac{df}{dr}>c_{1}>0
, for some strictly positive constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})>0
.

Therefore, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
associated to
Q^{f}+Q^{h}
. by summing the two currents
Q^{h},Q^{f}
we obtain the identity

(16.93)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(h+2f^{\prime}\right)|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=Q^{f}(\infty)-Q^{f}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(-h\real(u\bar{H})-\real(2f\bar{H}u^{\prime}+f^{\prime}\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star},

which, by using Lemmata
16.2
,
16.3
and by taking
\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
sufficiently large, we obtain

(16.94)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1+\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{f}(\infty)-Q^{f}(-\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(-h\real(u\bar{H})-\real(2f\bar{H}u^{\prime}+f^{\prime}\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq f(\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)-f(-\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(-h\real(u\bar{H})-\real(2f\bar{H}u^{\prime}+f^{\prime}\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star}.

Now, we will absorb the boundary terms on the right hand side of equation (
16.94
). We choose smooth cut-off functions
\chi_{1},\penalty\ \chi_{2}
such that

(16.95)

\chi_{1}=\begin{cases}1,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{+},r_{V,\textit{max}}-\delta]\\
0,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{V,\textit{max}}+\delta,\bar{r}_{+}]\end{cases},\qquad\textit{supp}(\chi^{\prime}_{1})\subset(r_{V,\textit{max}}-\frac{\delta}{2},r_{V,\textit{max}}+\frac{\delta}{2}),

(16.96)

\chi_{2}=\begin{cases}0,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{+},r_{V,\textit{max}}-\delta]\\
1,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{V,\textit{max}}+\delta,\bar{r}_{+}]\end{cases},\qquad\textit{supp}(\chi^{\prime}_{2})\subset(r_{V,\textit{max}}-\frac{\delta}{2},r_{V,\textit{max}}+\frac{\delta}{2}).

By using the fundamental theorem of calculus the following inequality holds

(16.97)

\displaystyle\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})|u|^{2}\right)(r_{+})\leq E_{0}\Big|\int_{r_{V,max}-\delta/2}^{r_{V,max}+\delta/2}\chi_{1}^{\prime}(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u})dr\Big|+E_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{1}(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})\imaginary(H\bar{u})dr

where we also have the bound

(16.98)

\displaystyle E_{0}\Big|\int_{r_{V,max}-\delta/2}^{r_{V,max}+\delta/2}\chi_{1}^{\prime}(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}})\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u})dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq E_{0}\delta^{-1}\int_{r_{V,max}-\delta/2}^{r_{V,max}+\delta/2}\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+m^{2})|u|^{2}\right)dr

\displaystyle\ll A\int_{r_{V,max}-\delta/2}^{r_{V,max}+\delta/2}\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+m^{2})|u|^{2}\right)dr,

where we choose
E_{0}\delta^{-1}\ll\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\epsilon}\omega^{2}_{high}
and
A=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\epsilon}\omega^{2}_{high}
. Of course, a similar estimate holds for
\chi_{2}
.

Finally, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.99)

-E_{0}\chi_{1}Q^{K^{+}}-E_{0}\chi_{2}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}},

for a sufficiently large
E_{0}>0
, such that in view of Proposition
6.4
we bound the boundary terms of (
16.94
) in view of (
16.97
) and by using the quantitative non trapping inequality that holds for the enlarged high superradiant frequencies, see the first inequality of (
16.79
).

We conclude the Proposition
16.2
.
∎

16.5.
Multipliers for the bounded frequency regime
F_{\flat}

We split the bounded frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}
, see Section
15
, into the following near stationary cases

(16.100)

\displaystyle\bullet\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}\quad\text{and}\quad|a|\leq a_{0}

\displaystyle\bullet\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|=0\}

\displaystyle\bullet\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}\quad\text{and}\quad|a|\geq a_{0}

and the non-stationary case

(16.101)

\{|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}

where

(16.102)

a_{0},\qquad\omega_{\textit{low}}>0,

are both sufficiently small, and are chosen in Propositions
16.3
,
16.5
respectively.

16.5.1.
Near stationary case

\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|\geq 1\}
and
\{|a|\leq a_{0}\}

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.3
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\lambda_{\textit{low}},\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
be arbitrary. Then, for any

(16.103)

a_{0}(M,l,\mu_{KG})>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{low}}>0,

both sufficiently small we have that if

(16.104)

|a|\leq a_{0}

then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low})\cap\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}
, there exist sufficiently regular functions
h,y
, where
y
is piecewise
C^{1}
, satisfying the uniform bound

(16.105)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B,

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.106)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Delta\big(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|\Psi|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\right|+\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|+\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|\Bigg)dr^{\star}.

We recall from Lemma
14.2
that

(16.107)

\tilde{V}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\frac{(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2m\omega a\Xi)\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\Delta\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}.

Note the following Lemma on the behaviour of
\tilde{V}
in a neighborhood of the infinities.

Lemma 16.4
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Then, for any

(16.108)

a_{0}^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{low}}^{-1}>0,\qquad R^{\star}>0

all sufficiently large, then if
|a|\leq a_{0}
then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}
we have

(16.109)

\displaystyle\tilde{V}^{\prime}<-b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur^{\star}\in(R^{\star},\infty),\qquad\tilde{V}^{\prime}>b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur^{\star}\in(-\infty,-R^{\star}).

(16.110)

|\tilde{V}|\leq\Delta\tilde{\lambda}+(a_{0}m)^{2}+\omega_{low}^{2},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur^{\star}\in(R^{\star},\infty),

where for the potential
\tilde{V}
see (
16.107
).

Moreover, we have that

(16.111)

\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Proof.

From Lemma
6.1
we note that
|m|>0
implies that
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, for
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small

For
\omega_{\textit{low}}
sufficiently small Lemma
6.1
implies the following

(16.112)

\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi>b>0.

Therefore, again it is immediate that for any
R^{\star}
sufficiently large, we differentiate the potential
\tilde{V}
, see (
16.107
), and obtain the desired result (
16.109
), for
a_{0},\penalty\ \omega_{\textit{low}}>0
sufficiently small.

Then, it is easy to see that for
\omega_{low}>0
sufficiently small we obtain inequality (
16.110
) by inspecting (
16.107
).

We conclude the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Now, we proceed to construct the multipliers
h,y
of Proposition
16.3
. For a graphic representation see Figure
3
, where note that the (red) graph that vanishes at the horizons corresponds to the function

Figure 3.
The red graph is
h
, the green graph is
y

First, we construct a piecewise
C^{1}
multiplier
y
in
(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)
and employ a
Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}
current, see Definition
14.2
.

Lemma 16.5
.

Let
R^{\star}>0
be sufficiently large and let
A_{\textit{large}}>0
. Then, there exists a piecewise
C^{1}
multiplier
y
such that the following hold.

For
r^{\star}\in(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)
we have

(16.113)

\displaystyle(Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\frac{r_{+}+\bar{r}_{+}}{2}\right)^{2}+a^{2}\right)\frac{4r\Delta(r)}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(4r(r^{2}+a^{2})\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}(\omega^{2}-\tilde{V})-\left((r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-\left(\left(\frac{r_{+}+\bar{r}_{+}}{2}\right)^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{2}\right)\tilde{V}^{\prime}\right)|\Psi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right),

where for the current
Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}
see Definition
14.2
.

For
r^{\star}\in(-\infty,-R^{\star}+1)\cup(R^{\star}-1,\infty)
we have

(16.114)

\frac{dy}{dr}-\frac{4r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}y=A_{\textit{large}}>0.

Proof.

Let
A_{\textit{large}}>0
be any positive number and let
R^{\star}>0
be sufficiently large. In the region
r^{\star}\in(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)
we define the multiplier

(16.115)

y=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-\left(\left(\frac{r_{+}+\bar{r}_{+}}{2}\right)^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{2},

which satisfies (
16.113
).

In the regions
r^{\star}\in(-\infty,-R^{\star}+1)
and
r^{\star}\in(R^{\star}-1,\infty)
we define respectively

(16.116)

y(r^{\star}(r))=\left(\frac{y}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(r_{2})(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}+A_{\textit{large}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\int_{r_{2}}^{r}\frac{dr}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}},

(16.117)

y(r^{\star}(r))=\left(\frac{y}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(r_{1})(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}+A_{\textit{large}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{dr}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}},

where
r_{1},r_{2}
are such that
r^{\star}(r_{2})=-R^{\star}-1,\penalty\ r^{\star}(r_{1})=R^{\star}-1
where note that for both (
16.116
), (
16.117
) the equation (
16.114
) holds.
∎

We construct a multiplier
h
in order to employ a
Q^{h}_{\textit{stat}}
current, see Definition
14.2
.

Lemma 16.6
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
R^{\star}>0
be any sufficiently large positive number, and let the multiplier
y
be as in Lemma
16.5
. Then, for any

(16.118)

a_{0}(M,l,\mu_{KG})>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{low}}>0,

both sufficiently small such that if
|a|\leq a_{0}
then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}
and for any sufficiently large constant

(16.119)

C_{\textit{large}}(a,a_{0},M,l,\mu_{KG},y|_{(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)},\omega_{low})>0

there exists a smooth
h
multiplier, satisfying the uniform bound

(16.120)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|\leq B(\omega_{low},a_{0}),

for all
r^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}
and
h\equiv 0
in neighborhoods of
\pm\infty
, and we have

(16.121)

h=C_{\textit{large}}\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{r^{2}+a^{2}},\qquad y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-(y\tilde{V})^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})\geq b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad y^{\prime}-\frac{4r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}y+h\geq b\Delta,

for
r^{\star}\in(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)
.

Proof.

Consider a

(16.122)

h=C_{\textit{large}}\tilde{\lambda}\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}

multiplier, where
C_{\textit{large}}>0
, and note

(16.123)

g(r)\equiv\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\implies-\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}g\right)^{\prime}\equiv 0.

Therefore, for any
a_{0}(M,l,\mu_{KG})>0
,
\omega_{\textit{low}}(a_{0},M,l,\mu_{KG})>0
both sufficiently small, we obtain the following

(16.124)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})

\displaystyle=C_{\textit{large}}\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{\lambda}(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})

\displaystyle=C_{\textit{large}}\frac{1}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\tilde{\lambda}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi)-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq C_{\textit{large}}b\tilde{\lambda},

for
r^{\star}\in(-R^{\star}+1,R^{\star}-1)
, where
C_{\textit{large}}>0
is a frequency independent constant. In the last inequality of (
16.124
) we used Lemma
16.4
and also took
\omega_{\textit{low}},\penalty\ a_{0}>0
sufficiently small.

Therefore if we add the current
Q^{y}
and take
C_{\textit{large}}>0
sufficiently large we conclude the first inequality of (
16.121
). We also readily obtain the last inequality of (
16.121
) by recalling the construction of
y
in Lemma
16.5
.

We extend
h
in
(-\infty,-R^{\star}+1)\cup(R^{\star}-1,\infty)
such that
h=0
in open neighborhoods of
\pm\infty
, with

(16.125)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|\leq B,

for all
r^{\star}
in a straightforward manner.
∎

We need the following Lemma

Lemma 16.7
.

Let the constants
C_{large},\penalty\ A_{large}>0
be both sufficiently large, where moreover
A_{large}\geq C_{large}^{2}
.

Then, the following hold

(16.126)

y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-(y\tilde{V})^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\qquad y^{\prime}-\frac{4r\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}y+h\geq b\Delta,

for
r^{\star}\in(-\infty,-R^{\star}-1)\cup(R^{\star}-1,\infty)
.

Proof.

First, we note from Lemma
16.6
, that the multiplier
h
satisfies

(16.127)

h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}\geq 0,\qquad r^{\star}\in(-R^{\star}+1,-R^{\star})\cup(R^{\star}-1,R^{\star}),

(16.128)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|\leq B,\quad r^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}.

We prove the first inequality of (
16.126
) in
(R^{\star},\infty)
. Similar estimates also conclude the first inequality of (
16.126
) in
(-\infty,-R^{\star})
.

We compute

(16.129)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-y^{\prime}\tilde{V}-y\tilde{V}^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\Big(\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}h\Big)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(\frac{dy}{dr}\omega^{2}-\frac{dy}{dr}\tilde{V}-y\frac{d\tilde{V}}{dr}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dr}\big(\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\frac{dh}{dr}\big)+\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}h+r\frac{d}{dr}\big(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\big)h+\frac{r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\frac{dh}{dr}\right)

\displaystyle+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2}).

In view of the construction of the multiplier
y
, see Lemma
16.5
and of the properties of the potential
\tilde{V}
, see Lemma
16.4
, we note that

(16.130)

\displaystyle\frac{dy}{dr}\omega^{2}-y\frac{d\tilde{V}}{dr}-\frac{dy}{dr}\tilde{V}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{4r}{r^{2}+a^{2}}y\right)\omega^{2}-\left(\left(\frac{y}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(r_{1})(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}+A_{\textit{large}}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{dr}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\frac{d\tilde{V}}{dr}-\frac{dy}{dr}\tilde{V}

\displaystyle\geq bA_{\textit{large}}\tilde{\lambda},

for
r^{\star}\in(R^{\star},+\infty]
. In the last inequality of (
16.130
) we took
a_{0},\omega_{\textit{low}}>0
both sufficiently small. Therefore, we obtain

(16.131)

y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-(yV)^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}\geq b(\epsilon)\Delta\tilde{\lambda},

in
(R^{\star},\infty]
, where we take
A_{\textit{large}}(C_{\textit{large}})>0
larger if needed.

We conclude that after taking
a_{0},\omega_{\textit{low}}
both sufficiently small, and
A_{\textit{large}}(C_{\textit{large}})
sufficiently larger if needed, we obtain that

(16.132)

y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-(y\tilde{V})^{\prime}+h(\tilde{V}-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+\left(\frac{r\Delta h}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\prime}\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},

for
r^{\star}\in(R^{\star},\infty)
, and conclude the first inequality of (
16.126
). We also readily conclude the second inequality of (
16.126
).
∎

The currents
y,h
have now been constructed.

Proof of Proposition
16.3
.

By combining the Lemmata
16.5
,
16.6
,
16.7
we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.3
associated with the current

(16.133)

Q^{y}_{stat}+Q^{h}_{stat}

and obtain

(16.134)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\big(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+1)|\Psi|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}+\int_{-R^{\star}}^{R^{\star}}C_{\textit{large}}\big(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|\Psi|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{y}[\Psi](+\infty)-Q^{y}[\Psi](-\infty)+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)+\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq By(\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|\Psi|^{2}(\infty)-By(-\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|\Psi|^{2}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|+\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|\right)dr^{\star},

where we utilized that
h(r^{\star})\equiv 0
in open neighborhoods of
-\infty,\infty
respectively, see Lemma
16.6
. Moreover, we used that
\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+(a\omega)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
from Lemma
16.4
.

Now, we need to absorb the boundary terms on the right hand side of inequality (
16.134
). Let
\chi(r^{\star})
be a smooth function such that
\chi=1
in
(-\infty,-R^{\star})
and
\chi=0
in
(R^{\star},\infty)
. Moreover, let
\bar{\chi}(r^{\star})
be a smooth function such that
\bar{\chi}(r^{\star})=0
in
(-\infty,-R^{\star})
and
\bar{\chi}(r^{\star})=1
in
(R^{\star},\infty)
. Now, in view of Proposition
6.4
, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.135)

-E_{0}\chi(r^{\star})Q^{K^{+}}[u]-E_{0}\bar{\chi}(r^{\star})Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]

for a sufficiently large
E_{0}
, and the we sum it in inequality (
16.134
). By taking
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small and
\omega_{\textit{low}}>0
sufficiently small (if necessary) we obtain

(16.136)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\big(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|\Psi|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}+\int_{-R^{\star}}^{R^{\star}}C_{\textit{large}}\big(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell})|\Psi|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\cdot E_{0}\int_{-R^{\star}}^{R^{\star}}\left(|\chi^{\prime}Q^{K^{+}}[u]|+|\bar{\chi}^{\prime}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|+\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

Then, to absorb the terms generated at
(-R^{\star},R^{\star})
, generated by the cut-offs
\chi,\bar{\chi}
, namely

(16.137)

E_{0}\int_{-R^{\star}}^{R^{\star}}\left(|\chi^{\prime}Q^{K^{+}}[u]|+|\bar{\chi}^{\prime}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]|\right)dr^{\star}\leq B\cdot E_{0}\int_{-R^{\star}}^{R^{\star}}\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{\tilde{\epsilon}}\right)|\Psi|^{2}+\tilde{\epsilon}|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}\right)dr^{\star},

for a sufficiently small
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
, we take the constant
C_{\textit{large}}>0
sufficiently larger if needed, see Lemma
16.6
, and absorb them.

Thus the proof of Proposition
16.3
is complete.
∎

16.5.2.
Near stationary case

\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|=0\}

In view of the definition of the frequency regimes, see Definition
15.1
, this frequency regime is manifestly non-superradiant.

Remark 16.3
.

Note that Proposition
16.4
is the only main Proposition of Section
16
where in fact the LHS of (
16.140
) does not control the lower order terms
|\Psi|^{2}
in the bulk of the left hand side in the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.4
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\lambda_{\textit{low}},\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
be arbitrary. For any

(16.138)

\omega_{\textit{low}}>0

sufficiently small, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}(\lambda_{low},\omega_{high})\cap\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|=0\}
, there exist sufficiently regular functions
h,y
satisfying the uniform bound

(16.139)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B,

where
y
is piecewise
C^{1}
, such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.140)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Delta\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{0\ell})|\Psi|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(\left|\omega\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\right|+\left|\omega\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\right|+\mu^{2}_{KG}\left|\real\left(\frac{2\Psi h\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|+\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|\Bigg)dr^{\star}.

First, we note that following lemma that for the case of axisymmetry
m=0
the potential
\tilde{V}
, see Lemma
14.2
, can be written as

(16.141)

\tilde{V}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\dot{=}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\frac{\tilde{\lambda}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\Delta\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

We are ready to prove the main Proposition

Proof of Proposition
16.4
.

First, we discuss the case
\mu^{2}_{KG}>0
. We note that we may apply the same multipliers that we used in the previous Section
16.5.1
, where recall that we treated the near stationary regime
\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{low}\}\cap\{|m|>0\}
and
|a|\leq a_{0}
, and conclude the desired result (
16.140
) for any
\omega_{low}>0
sufficiently small.

Now, we continue with the more elaborate case
\mu^{2}_{KG}=0
. Let
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
be a sufficiently small real number. First, it is immediate from Lemma
6.1
that
\tilde{\lambda}\geq 0
for
m=0
. We consider the three cases

(1)

\tilde{\lambda}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}\omega^{2}

(2)

\tilde{\lambda}\geq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}}\omega^{2}

(3)

\epsilon^{\prime}\omega^{2}\leq\tilde{\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon^{\prime}}\omega^{2}
.

In the first case
we proceed as follows. We define the multiplier

(16.142)

y=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}

and we calculate

(16.143)

y^{\prime}-y\frac{4r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}=2r(r^{2}+a^{2})\Delta\geq b\Delta.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.3
, associated to
Q^{y}_{stat}
, and obtain that for any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
sufficiently small the following holds

(16.144)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta\omega^{2}|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}\leq B|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi])^{\prime}dr^{\star}|+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|dr^{\star}.

In view of the fact that this frequency regime is axisymmetric and thus non-superradiant, we integrate the energy estimate of Lemma
14.4
, associated with the current
-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}[u]-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u]
for a sufficiently large
E_{0}>0
, in view of Proposition
6.4
, and then we sum it in (
16.144
), and conclude the desired (
16.140
).

In the second case
we can again use the same multipliers that we used in the previous Section
16.5.1
, for any sufficiently small
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
,
\omega_{low}>0
. Specifically, see Lemmata
16.5
,
16.6
,
16.7
for the construction of the multipliers. To ease the comparison, recall that in Section
16.5.1
we used that
\lambda\gg\omega^{2}
to construct the multipliers
h,y
. The same arguments can be carried over to the present proof with the assumption of axisymmetry
m=0
, instead of slow rotation
a_{0}\ll 1
.

At this point we fix the
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.

In the third case
we proceed as follows. Again we define

(16.145)

y=(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.3
, associated to
Q^{y}_{stat}
to write

(16.146)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\Big(y^{\prime}-y\frac{4r\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\Big)|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+\Big(y^{\prime}\omega^{2}-(y\tilde{V})^{\prime}\Big)|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}(Q^{y}_{\textit{stat}}[\Psi])^{\prime}dr^{\star}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)dr^{\star}.

By integration by parts and by using that
\tilde{V}(r_{+})=\tilde{V}(\bar{r}_{+})=0
we obtain

(16.147)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+y^{\prime}\omega^{2}|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq-Q^{y}_{stat}[\Psi](+\infty)+Q^{y}_{stat}[\Psi](-\infty)+\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\tilde{\lambda}^{2}|\Psi|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)dr^{\star},

where we also used a Young’s ineqality. By taking
\epsilon>0,\omega_{low}>0
both sufficiently small, since
\tilde{\lambda}^{2}\sim\omega^{4}
, we obtain the result

(16.148)

b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\Delta|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+y^{\prime}\omega^{2}|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}\leq-Q^{y}_{stat}[\Psi](+\infty)+Q^{y}_{stat}[\Psi](-\infty)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\overline{H^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)dr^{\star}.

Finally, in view of the fact that this frequency regime is axisymmetric and thus non-superradiant, we integrate the energy identities of Lemmata
14.3
,
14.4
associated with the following current

(16.149)

Q_{\textit{stat}}^{y}[\Psi]-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}[u]-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}[u],

in view of Proposition
6.4
, for a sufficiently large
E_{0}>0
, and conclude

(16.150)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Delta\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{0\ell})|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg(\left|\omega\imaginary(\bar{\Psi}H)\right|+\left|\real\left(\frac{y\Psi^{\prime}\bar{H}}{\sqrt{r^{2}+a^{2}}}\right)\right|\Bigg)dr^{\star}.

We conclude the result of the Proposition. ∎

16.5.3.
Near stationary case

\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|\geq 1\}
and
\{|a|\geq a_{0}\}

In view of the definition of the frequency regimes, see Definition
15.1
, this frequency regime is manifestly non-superradiant for a sufficiently small
\omega_{\textit{low}}>0
.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.5
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\omega_{high},\lambda_{low}>0
. Let
|a|\geq a_{0}
. Then, for

(16.151)

-\infty<r^{\star}_{-\infty}<0<r^{\star}_{+\infty}<+\infty

sufficiently small (and negative) and sufficiently large (and positive) respectively, and for any

(16.152)

\omega_{\textit{low}}>0,

sufficiently small, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low})\cap\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}
there exists a multiplier
y
satisfying the uniform bound

(16.153)

|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B(\lambda_{\textit{low}},\omega_{\textit{high}})

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.154)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{+\infty}}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

First, note the following Lemma

Lemma 16.8
.

Let the assumptions of Proposition
16.5
hold. Then the following hold

(16.155)

\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\geq b|m|^{2},\qquad\omega^{2}-V(r_{+})\geq b|m|^{2},\qquad\omega^{2}-V(\bar{r}_{+})\geq b|m|^{2}.

Proof.

This is straightforward.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
16.5
.

Proof of Proposition
16.5
.

For simplicity, we define the potential
\tilde{V}
such that

(16.156)

\omega^{2}-V=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-\tilde{V}

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
, associated to
Q^{y}
, to get

(16.157)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+y^{\prime}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=Q^{y}[u](\infty)-Q^{y}[u](-\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(y\tilde{V}\right)^{\prime}|u|^{2}+2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right)dr^{\star}.

Consider the multiplier

(16.158)

y=-\exp(-C\int^{r^\star}_{-\infty} \chi_{r^\star_{\infty}}),

such that
\chi_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}
satisfies the following

(16.159)

\chi_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}(r)=\begin{cases}\text{$1$}&\quad\text{if}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{+},r_{\infty}-1)\\
\text{$0$}&\quad\text{if}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r^{\star}_{\infty},\bar{r}_{+}),\\
\end{cases}

for
r^{\star}_{\infty}
sufficiently large.

We note the following straightforward computation

(16.160)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(y\tilde{V}\right)^{\prime}|u|^{2}dr^{\star}\leq\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+B\epsilon^{-1}C^{-2}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}dr^{\star}.

Therefore, from (
16.157
), (
16.160
) we obtain

(16.161)

\displaystyle b\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+y^{\prime}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{y}[u](r^{\star}=-\infty)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq y(-\infty)\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(\omega^{2}-V\right)\right)(r^{\star}=-\infty)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})dr^{\star}.

As noted earlier, for
|a|\geq a_{0}
, the present frequency regime

(16.162)

\{|\omega|\leq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}\cap\{|m|\geq 1\}

is non-superradiant, namely for
a_{0}>0
as in Proposition
16.3
and for a sufficiently small

(16.163)

\omega_{\textit{low}}(a,M,l,\mu_{KG})

we obtain

(16.164)

\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\cdot\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)>0.

Therefore, in view of Proposition
6.4
, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.165)

-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}

for a sufficiently large
E_{0}
, we sum it in (
16.161
) and we obtain the result.
∎

16.5.4.
Non stationary case

\{|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.6
.

Let
l>0

(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let

(16.166)

\omega_{\textit{low}}>0,\qquad\lambda_{\textit{low}}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0

be any positive real numbers and let
\mathcal{C}>1
be sufficiently large. Then, for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}(\omega_{low},\omega_{high},\lambda_{low})\cap\{|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{low}}\}
and for any

(16.167)

-\infty<r^{\star}_{-\infty}<0<r^{\star}_{+\infty}<+\infty

sufficiently small (and negative) and sufficiently large (and positive) respectively, there exists a multiplier
y
satisfying the uniform bound

(16.168)

|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B(\lambda_{\textit{low}},\omega_{\textit{high}},\mathcal{C})

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have the following:

If in addition
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{SF}\cap\left(\{|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\geq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\geq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\right)
then we have

(16.169)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{+\infty}}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B(r^{\star}_{\pm\infty},\mathcal{C})\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right||u|^{2}(-\infty)\Big),

and if in addition
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\left(\{|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\mathcal{SF}^{c}\right)
then we have

(16.170)

\displaystyle\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{+\infty}}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B(r^{\star}_{\pm\infty},\mathcal{C})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\Big)dr^{\star}.

Proof.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
, associated to
Q^{y}
, to get

(16.171)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+y^{\prime}\omega^{2}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}=Q^{y}[u](\infty)-Q^{y}[u](-\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left((yV)^{\prime}|u|^{2}+2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right)dr^{\star}.

Let
\epsilon>0
be sufficiently small. Consider the multiplier

(16.172)

y=-\exp(-C\int^{r^\star}_{-\infty} \chi_{r^\star_{\infty}}),

such that
\chi_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}
satisfies the following

(16.173)

\chi_{r^{\star}_{\infty}}(r)=\begin{cases}\text{$1$}&\quad\text{if}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{+},r_{\infty}-\epsilon))\\
\text{$0$}&\quad\text{if}\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{\infty},\bar{r}_{+}),\\
\end{cases}

for
r^{\star}_{\infty}>0
sufficiently large. By using the definition of the multiplier
y
, in equation (
16.172
), we note the following

(16.174)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(yV)^{\prime}|u|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=\big[yV|u|^{2}\big]_{-\infty}^{\infty}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}yV(u\bar{u}^{\prime}+u^{\prime}\bar{u})dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\left(yV|u|^{2}\right)(r^{\star}=\infty)-\left(yV|u|^{2}\right)(r^{\star}=-\infty)-\int_{\mathbb{R}}|yV|(u\bar{u}^{\prime}+u^{\prime}\bar{u})dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\left(\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(y|u|^{2}\right)(\infty)-B\left(\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(y|u|^{2}\right)(-\infty)

\displaystyle+\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{2}\frac{V^{2}}{y^{\prime}}|u|^{2}dr^{\star}.

Observe now that
y^{\prime}>0
. So, the first bulk term on the on the right hand side of the inequality (
16.174
) will be absorbed, in the left hand side of equation (
16.171
), by taking
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. The last bulk term on the right hand side of (
16.174
), will be absorbed, in the left hand side of equation (
16.171
), by taking
C>0
sufficiently large. We thus obtain the energy estimate

(16.175)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq BQ^{y}(\infty)-BQ^{y}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\left(\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(y|u|^{2}\right)(\infty)-B\left(\omega^{2}-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\right)\left(y|u|^{2}\right)(-\infty)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\real(u^{\prime}H)dr^{\star}.

Remark 16.4
.

Note that the intersection
\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{am\omega\in\left(\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}},\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\}
is in general not empty, see Remark
15.2
, and therefore the boundary terms on the right hand side of (
16.175
), in general, have different signs.

We proceed as follows. In the superradiant frequencies

(16.176)

(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{SF}

we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current
-EQ^{K^{+}}-EQ^{\bar{K}^{+}}
, where
E
is sufficiently large. We sum it to we use estimate (
16.175
), and take
E
sufficiently large so that we do absorb the boundary term
Q^{y}(\infty)
and we conclude the following

(16.177)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right||u|^{2}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

In the non superradiant frequencies

(16.178)

(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\mathcal{SF}^{c}

we can remove the boundary term of (
16.177
) by summing in (
16.175
) an energy identity associates with a current of the form
E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}+E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}
and obtain

(16.179)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

Now, assume that

(16.180)

(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\left(\{|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\{|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}\}\cup\mathcal{SF}\right)

for a sufficiently large
\mathcal{C}>0
. In what follows we only discuss the case
|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}
, since the case
|\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}
admits an almost identical treatment. Let
\chi
be a smooth function with

(16.181)

\chi=\begin{cases}1,\qquad r\in[r_{+},2r_{-\infty}]\\
0,\qquad r\in[3r_{-\infty},\bar{r}_{+}].\end{cases}

Let
Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}
be the currents of Definition
14.3
. By using the fundamental theorem of calculus for
\chi\cdot 1_{|\omega-\omega_{+}m|\leq\mathcal{C}^{-1}}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}
we obtain

(16.182)

|\omega-\omega_{+}m||\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m||u|^{2}(-\infty)\leq\int_{2r_{-\infty}^{\star}}^{3r_{-\infty}^{\star}}|\omega-\omega_{+}m||\chi^{\prime}\imaginary(u^{\prime}\bar{u})|dr^{\star}+\int_{-\infty}^{3r^{\star}_{\infty}}|\chi||\omega-\omega_{+}m||\imaginary(H\bar{u})|dr^{\star}.

Now, by taking
\mathcal{C}
sufficiently large, we use (
16.182
) in (
16.177
) and obtain the desired result

(16.183)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}y^{\prime}\big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+\left|\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}.

Therefore, from (
16.183
), (
16.179
), (
16.177
) we conclude (
16.169
), (
16.170
) and the proof.

We conclude the proof.
∎

16.6.
Multipliers for the
\lambda
dominated frequency regime
F_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}

In view of the definition of the frequency regimes, see Definition
15.1
, this frequency regime is manifestly non-superradiant.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.7
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. For

(16.184)

\alpha^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,\qquad\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}>0

all sufficiently large, where moreover
\lambda_{low}\ll\alpha^{2}
, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}(\lambda_{low},\omega_{high},\alpha)
there exist sufficiently regular functions
f,h
satisfying the uniform bounds

(16.185)

|f|+|f^{\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime}|+|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|\leq B(\lambda_{\textit{low}},\omega_{\textit{high}})

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.186)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\Bigg(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\Delta(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\Bigg)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(u\bar{H})\right|+\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(u\bar{H})\right|

\displaystyle+\left|2fRe(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right|+(|f^{\prime}|+|h|)\left|Re(\bar{H}u)\right|\Big)dr^{\star}.

Note the following lemma for the potential
V
.

Lemma 16.9
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
.

(16.187)

\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,\qquad\lambda^{-1}_{\textit{low}}>0,\qquad\alpha^{-1}>0

be sufficiently large, where
\lambda_{low}\ll\alpha^{2}
. Then, for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}(\lambda_{low},\omega_{high},\alpha)
the potential
V
attains a unique critical point, a maximum, at a value

(16.188)

r_{V,\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}).

and moreover there exist
r^{\star}_{-\infty}<0<r^{\star}_{+\infty}
sufficiently negative and sufficiently positive respectively, such that

(16.189)

\displaystyle b\Delta\tilde{\lambda}\leq V^{\prime}\leq B\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\quad r^{\star}<r^{\star}_{-\infty},

\displaystyle b\Delta\tilde{\lambda}\leq-V^{\prime}\leq B\Delta\tilde{\lambda},\quad r^{\star}>r^{\star}_{\infty}.

Finally, the following holds

(16.190)

|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Proof.

First, by recalling the definition of the potential
V
, see (
6.26
), we note that

(16.191)

\frac{dV}{dr}=\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}+\frac{dV_{\textit{SL}}}{dr}+\frac{dV_{\mu}}{dr},

where

(16.192)

\displaystyle\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)-2\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\frac{2r}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}am\Xi

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)-\frac{4r}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(am\omega\Xi)+\frac{4r}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}}(am\Xi)^{2}.

Note that for a sufficiently small
\alpha(a,M,l)>0
and for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}
we may assume

(16.193)

am\omega\leq 0.

Suppose the opposite, namely that
am\omega>0
. Then, we have

(16.194)

am\omega\geq\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\geq|a|\alpha\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\omega^{2}\implies|m|\geq\alpha\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}|\omega|.

On the other hand the following holds

(16.195)

am\omega\geq\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\implies|\omega|\geq\alpha\frac{\tilde{\lambda}}{|m|}\geq\alpha\Xi|m|,

where we also used (
6.8
) from Lemma
6.1
. Combining (
16.194
), (
16.195
) we obtain

(16.196)

|\omega|\geq\alpha^{2}\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\Xi|\omega|.\implies\lambda_{low}\geq\alpha^{2}\Xi.

Therefore, for a sufficiently small
\alpha>0
if we take
\lambda_{low}(\alpha)\sim\alpha^{3}>0
sufficiently small we obtain a contradiction. Therefore, we conclude that
am\omega\leq 0
.

Now, since
am\omega\leq 0
, then from (
16.192
) we obtain

(16.197)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}\implies(r^{2}+a^{2})^{3}\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r=r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}.

which immediately implies that
\frac{dV}{dr}(r_{+})\geq b\tilde{\lambda}
, by taking
\lambda_{low}^{-1}
sufficiently large.

Therefore, by taking
\lambda_{low}^{-1}
sufficiently large, and in view of the fact that
\tilde{\lambda}>\lambda_{low}^{-1}(\omega^{2}+a^{2}m^{2})
we obtain that indeed
V
attains a unique maximum
r_{V,max}
and moreover (
16.189
), (
16.190
) hold. ∎

Proof of Proposition
16.7
.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
, associated to
Q^{f}+Q^{h}
, to get

(16.198)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2f^{\prime}+h\right)|u^{\prime}|^{2}dr^{\star}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)|u|^{2}dr^{\star}

\displaystyle=Q^{f}(\infty)-Q^{f}(-\infty)+Q^{h}(\infty)-Q^{h}(-\infty)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})+f^{\prime}\real(u\bar{H})+h\real(u\bar{H})\right)dr^{\star}.

Specifically, for a sufficiently small
p>0
we consider the following regions

(16.199)

-\infty<e^{p^{-1}}r^{\star}_{-\infty}<r^{\star}_{-\infty}<0<r^{\star}_{+\infty}<e^{p^{-1}}r^{\star}_{+\infty}<\infty,

where
r^{\star}_{-\infty}<0<r^{\star}_{+\infty}
are from Lemma
16.9
.

First, we construct the multiplier

(16.200)

f=\frac{2}{\pi}\arctan(r-r_{V,\textit{max}})

for all
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. Note that
\frac{df}{dr}>0
in
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
and

(16.201)

-fV^{\prime}\geq b\Delta(r-r_{V,\textit{max}})^{2}\left(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}\right),\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}].

Now, to deal with the region away from the horizons, we construct the multiplier
h
such that

(16.202)

h=\begin{cases}0,\quad r^{\star}\in(-\infty,e^{p^{-1}}r^{\star}_{-\infty})\cup(e^{p^{-1}}r^{\star}_{+\infty},\bar{r}_{+})\\
1,\quad r^{\star}\in(r_{-\infty},r_{+\infty}),\end{cases}

and
h(r)\geq 0
for
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
.

Now, the integrand in the left hand side of (
16.198
) satisfies the following

(16.203)

-fV^{\prime}+h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b\Delta\left(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}+\omega^{2}+1\right),\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}],

by requiring
\lambda_{\textit{low}}
smaller if needed, in view of Lemma
16.9
. Moreover, note
h(\pm\infty)=0
, therefore we obtain

(16.204)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\Bigg(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(1+\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}+\omega^{2})|u|^{2}\Bigg)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq BQ^{f}(\infty)-BQ^{f}(-\infty)+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2fRe(u^{\prime}\bar{H})+(f^{\prime}+h)Re(\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Bf(\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)-Bf(-\infty)\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2fRe(u^{\prime}\bar{H})+(f^{\prime}+h)Re(\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star}.

Finally, in view of the fact that the
\lambda
–dominated frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{{{\mbox{$\flat\mkern-12.0mu{}^{\_}$}}}}
is non-superradiant, see Section
15
, then, in view of Proposition
6.4
, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.205)

-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}

and sum it to inequality (
16.204
) to conclude the desired result.
∎

16.7.
Multipliers for the high
\omega\sim\lambda
frequency regime
F_{\natural}

In view of the definition of the frequency regimes, see Definition
15.1
, this frequency regime is manifestly non-superradiant.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.8
.

Let
l>0

(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\alpha>0
. For any

(16.206)

all sufficiently large, then for

and there exists a piecewise

multiplier

that satisfy the uniform bound

(16.207)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|y|+|y^{\prime}|+|f|+|f^{\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime\prime}|+|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|\leq B,

and there exitsts an

(16.208)

where
\epsilon_{away}(a,M,l)>0
is any sufficiently small real number, such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (

) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (

(16.209)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|+|f^{\prime}\real(\bar{H}u)|+|y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})|+|h\real(u\bar{H})|+\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+E\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)+\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{u}H)\right)dr^{\star}.

Remark 16.5
.

If the solution
\psi
of the Klein–Gordon equation (
1.7
) is axisymmetric
\partial_{\varphi^{\star}}\psi=0
then we note that in Proposition
16.8
, we have
r_{\textit{trap}}=r_{\Delta,\textit{trap}}
, where
r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}
is the value of the unique local maximum of
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
, see Lemma
3.3
.

We note that for the present frequency regime
(\omega,m,\bar{\lambda})\in\mathcal{F}_{\natural}
the following hold:

•

if
am\omega<0
then
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}
,

•

if
am\omega>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha|a|\tilde{\lambda}
then
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}
.

Therefore, in view of Lemma
13.1
, it would be convenient to keep in mind that the potential
V_{0}
satisfies only one of the following

(16.210)

\displaystyle(V_{0}\penalty\ case\penalty\ 1)\penalty\ \text{There exists a unique critical point of}\penalty\ V_{0}\penalty\ \text{that satisfies}\penalty\ r_{+}<r_{V_{0},max}<\bar{r}_{+}

\displaystyle(V_{0}\penalty\ case\penalty\ 2)\penalty\ \text{There exist exist two critical points of}\penalty\ V_{0}\penalty\ \text{that satisfy}\penalty\ r_{+}<r_{V_{0},min}<r_{V_{0},max}<\bar{r}_{+}

\displaystyle(V_{0}\penalty\ case\penalty\ 3)\penalty\ \text{There exist exist two critical points of}\penalty\ V_{0}\penalty\ \text{that satisfy}\penalty\ r_{+}<r_{V_{0},min}<r_{V_{0},max}=\bar{r}_{+}

\displaystyle(V_{0}\penalty\ case\penalty\ 4)\penalty\ \frac{dV_{0}}{dr}>0\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \frac{dV_{0}}{dr}<0\penalty\ \textit{for all}\penalty\ r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}).

For a graphic representation of the above cases see Figure
4
. Note that in the
(Potential\penalty\ case\penalty\ 3)
we do not exclude that
V_{0}
attains a critical point at
\bar{r}_{+}
.
