The cases of (
16.210
) respectively.

Now, we construct the
Q^{f},Q^{y},Q^{h}
currents, see Definition
14.1
.

Lemma 16.10
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\alpha>0
. For any

(16.211)

\omega_{\textit{high}}>0,\qquad(\lambda_{low})^{-1}>0,\qquad\epsilon_{away}^{-1}>0

all sufficiently large then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\natural}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low},\alpha)
there exists a trapping parameter

(16.212)

r_{trap}(\omega,m,\ell)\in[r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away}]\cup\{0\},

where
\epsilon_{away}>0
, such that the following hold.

There exist smooth multiplier
f=f(\omega,m,\ell),h=h(\omega,m,\ell)
and there exists a piecewise
C^{1}
multiplier
y=y(\omega,m,\ell)
such that the following uniform bound holds piecewise

(16.213)

|h|+|h^{\prime}|+|h^{\prime\prime}|+|f|+|f^{\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime}|+|f^{\prime\prime\prime}|+|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low}),

and we have

(16.214)

\displaystyle(P1)\quad f(r_{\textit{trap}})=0,

\displaystyle(P2)\quad h+y^{\prime}+2f^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,\penalty\ r\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})

\displaystyle(P3)\quad\left(-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)+\left(y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\right)+\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\right)\geq b\Delta\cdot(r-r_{\textit{trap}})^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}+\omega^{2}\right)+b\Delta,\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mur\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}).

Proof.

We note that

(16.215)

\omega^{2}-V(r_{+})=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad\omega^{2}-V(\bar{r}_{+})=\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}

where in the inequalities above we used that
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\natural}
for sufficiently large
\omega_{high}>0
.

Let
\epsilon_{trap}>0
be sufficiently small. In view of the definition of the high
\omega\sim\lambda
frequency regime
\mathcal{F}_{\natural}
, see Definition
15.1
, we separate the frequency space
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\natural}
in the following sets

(1)

|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|\leq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
,
am\omega<0

(2)

|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|\leq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
,
am\omega\geq\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha|a|\tilde{\lambda}

(3)

|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|>\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
,
am\omega<0

(4)

|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|>\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
,
am\omega\geq\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha|a|\tilde{\lambda}

As will become apparent below, the first two sets above are subject to trapping, namely
r_{trap}\neq 0
.

\bullet
We begin with the first case
(1)
. In this case we define the multipliers
y,h\equiv 0
. First, we note that because
am\omega<0
we obtain

(16.216)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})>b\tilde{\lambda}.

Moreover, we easily conclude that

(16.217)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda},

in view of the fact that
|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|\leq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
and therefore the maximum value of
V_{0}
cannot be attained at the cosmological horizon
\bar{r}_{+}
, also see the graphs of Figure
4
. Now, in view of Lemma
13.1
, on the critical points of the potential
V_{0}
, we conclude that indeed there exists a unique critical point
r_{V_{0},max}\in(r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away})
of
V_{0}
which is a maximum and moreover the following holds
\frac{d^{2}V_{0}}{dr^{2}}(r)<-b\tilde{\lambda}
for
r\in(r_{V_{0}}-\epsilon_{away},r_{V_{0}}+\epsilon_{away})
. Now, we take
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large and
\epsilon_{away}>0
sufficiently small to conclude that

(16.218)

\displaystyle\frac{dV}{dr}>b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad r\in[r_{+},r_{V_{0}}-2^{-1}\epsilon_{away}]

\displaystyle\frac{dV}{dr}<-b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad r\in[r_{V_{0}}+2^{-1}\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}]

and moreover
\frac{d^{2}V}{dr^{2}}(r)<0
for
r\in[r_{V_{0}}-2^{-1}\epsilon_{away},r_{V_{0}}+2^{-1}\epsilon_{away}]
. Therefore, we obtain that the potential
V
attains a unique maximum at
r_{V,max}\in(r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away})
. In this case we define

(16.219)

r_{trap}=r_{V,max}.

In view of the above, we construct a multiplier
f
as follows

(16.220)

-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Delta,\qquad f(r_{trap})=0,\qquad f^{\prime}(r_{trap})=1,\qquad f^{\prime\prime}(r_{trap})=0,

in a neighborghood of
r_{trap}
. We extend
f
smoothly in the entire interval
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
such that we have

(16.221)

f^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,\qquad-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b\tilde{\lambda}\Delta(r-r_{trap})^{2}+b\Delta

and conclude (
16.214
).

\bullet
We continue with the second case
(2)
. We first define the multiplier
h\equiv 0
. Since
am\omega\geq\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha|a|\tilde{\lambda}
then we obtain
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}
. Moreover, in view of (
16.215
) we can find an
r_{3}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low},\epsilon_{trap})\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
such that

(16.222)

\omega^{2}-V\geq b\tilde{\lambda},\qquad r\in[r_{+},r_{3}].

It is an easy consequence of the trapping condition
|\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V-\omega^{2}|\leq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
and of Lemma
13.1
, on the critical points of
V_{0}
, that for any
\omega_{high},\lambda_{low}^{-1}>0
sufficiently large and for any
\epsilon_{trap}>0
sufficiently small we can have

(16.223)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{3})\geq b\tilde{\lambda},

the reader should also look at the second Figure
4
.

It is obvious now that, for sufficiently positive
\omega_{high}
, the critical point
r_{V,max}
is attained inside the interval
[r_{3},\bar{r}_{+}]
. We define

(16.224)

r_{trap}=r_{V,max}

Moreover, for sufficiently small
\epsilon_{trap},\penalty\ \epsilon_{away},\lambda_{low}>0
we conclude that
r_{trap}\in[r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away}]
and of course
r_{3}<\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away}
.

In view of the above, we will now construct two multipliers
y,f
. First, our multiplier
y
will satisfy the following

(16.225)

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=1-e^{C_{large}(r_{3}-r)},\quad r\in[r_{+},r_{3}],

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle=0,\qquad r\in[r_{3},\bar{r}_{+}],

where
C_{large}>0
will be chosen sufficiently large later. Note that

(16.226)

y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}C_{large}e^{C_{large}(r_{3}-r)}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\geq b(C_{large})\tilde{\lambda}

in the interval
(r_{+},r_{3})
,where in the last inequality we used that
\omega^{2}\sim\tilde{\lambda}
and we took
C_{large}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low})>0
sufficiently large.

We construct the multiplier
f
again by solving the initial value problem (
16.220
). We choose
C_{large}
sufficiently positive to obtain the following

(16.227)

\displaystyle 2f^{\prime}+y^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,

\displaystyle\left(y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\right)+\left(-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}\right)\geq b\tilde{\lambda}(r-r_{trap})^{2}\Delta+b\Delta,

which concludes (
16.214
).

\bullet
We continue with the third case
(3)
. In this case we set

(16.228)

r_{trap}=0.

We first define
y\equiv 0
. We note argue similarly to case
(1)
. Specifically, since
am\omega<0
we have that

(16.229)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{+})\geq b\tilde{\lambda}.

Therefore, by Lemma
13.1
, we take
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large and we find that there exists a maximum of the potential
V
, which we here call
r_{V,max}
such that
r_{V,max}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. Note that
r_{V,max}
is not necessarily a critical point and that it can be the case that
r_{V,max}=\bar{r}_{+}
.

We define a multiplier
f
by solving the initial value problem

(16.230)

-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Delta,\qquad f(r_{V,max})=0,\qquad f^{\prime}(r_{V,max})=1,\qquad f^{\prime\prime}(r_{V,max})=0,

in a neighborhood of
r_{V,max}
. Recall that we do not exclude the case
r_{V,max}=\bar{r}_{+}
. We extend
f
smoothly in the entire interval
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
such that

(16.231)

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,\qquad-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b\Delta(r-r_{trap})^{2}\tilde{\lambda}+b\Delta.

after taking
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large.

We use the non trapping condition
|\max_{r}V-\omega^{2}|\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
so that we can construct a multiplier
h
such that for sufficiently large
\omega_{high},\lambda_{low}^{-1}
and sufficiently small
\epsilon_{trap}>0
we find that

(16.232)

\left(h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}\right)+\left(-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime}\right)\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda},

which concludes (
16.214
).

\bullet
Finally, we study that fourth case
(4)
. We split the frequency space of case
(4)
into the two frequency regimes

(16.233)

\displaystyle\{\omega^{2}-\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}\}

\displaystyle\{\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}\}.

For the first case of (
16.233
) we note that
\omega^{2}-V(r)\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
for all
r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
. We define
h,f\equiv 0
and we define a multiplier of the form
y=e^{C_{large}r}
. For a sufficiently large
C_{large}>0
we obtain that

(16.234)

y^{\prime}(\omega-V)-yV^{\prime}\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda}.

We fix the
C_{large}>0
.

We now study the second case of (
16.233
). First, we note that since
am\omega>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}>\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}
and
am\omega\geq\alpha|a|\tilde{\lambda}
then we have that

(16.235)

\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(\bar{r}_{+})<-b\tilde{\lambda}.

We note that the maximum value of
V_{0}
cannot be attained at the event horizon since then we would have
-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
. Therefore, for any sufficiently small
\epsilon_{away}>0
and for any
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large we have that the potential
V
attaines a maximim

(16.236)

r_{V,max}\in(r_{+}+\epsilon_{away},\bar{r}_{+}-\epsilon_{away}).

Now, for any
\epsilon_{1}>0
sufficiently small we have that
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}(r_{V_{0},max}-\epsilon_{1})>b(\epsilon_{1})\tilde{\lambda}
. We define
r_{3}=r_{V_{0},max}-\epsilon_{1}
. We fix
\epsilon_{1}
.

We now construct three multipliers
y,f,h
as follows. First, we define
y
to be the following piecewise smooth function

(16.237)

y=\begin{cases}&1-e^{C_{1}(r_{3}-r)},\penalty\ r\in[r_{+},r_{3}]\\
&0,\penalty\ r\in[r_{3},\bar{r}_{+}],\end{cases}

for some
C_{1}>0
that will be chosen sufficiently large later. We define a multiplier
f
by solving the initial value problem

(16.238)

-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Delta,\qquad f(r_{V_{0},max})=0,\qquad f^{\prime}(r_{V_{0},max})=1,\qquad f^{\prime\prime}(r_{V_{0},max})=0,

in a neighborhood of
r_{V,max}
. We extend
f
smoothly in the entire interval
[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]
such that

(16.239)

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,\qquad-fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b\Delta(r-r_{V,max})^{2}\tilde{\lambda}.

We define the smooth multiplier
h
such that it satisfies the following

(16.240)

h=\begin{cases}&1,\qquad r\in[r_{V,max}-2\epsilon_{1},r_{V,max}+2\epsilon_{1}]\\
&0,\qquad r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]\setminus[r_{V,max}-2\epsilon_{1},r_{V,max}+2\epsilon_{1}].\end{cases}

Therefore, for a sufficiently large
C_{1}>0
then in view of the non trapping condition
\max V_{0}-\omega^{2}\geq\epsilon_{trap}\tilde{\lambda}
we obtain that for any
\omega_{high}>0
sufficiently large the following hold

(16.241)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}+2f^{\prime}+h\geq b\Delta

\displaystyle h(V-\omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}+y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}+fV^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}f^{\prime\prime\prime}\geq b\Delta\tilde{\lambda}.

We conclude Lemma
16.10
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
16.8
.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
associated to the current

(16.242)

Q^{f}+Q^{y}+Q^{h}

see Definition
14.1
, and use the construction of the
f,y
multipliers of Lemma
16.10
to obtain the energy inequality

(16.243)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left((r-r_{\textit{trap}})^{2}(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}+\omega^{2})\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{f}(\infty)-Q^{f}(-\infty)-B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})+f^{\prime}\real(\bar{H}u)\right)dr^{\star}+Q^{y}(\infty)-Q^{y}(-\infty)+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\Big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(\infty)+B\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}|u|^{2}(-\infty)

\displaystyle+B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|2f\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})\right|+\left|f^{\prime}\real(\bar{H}u)\right|+\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H\right|+\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|+|h\real(u\bar{H})|\right)dr^{\star}.

Finally, since the high
\omega\sim\lambda
frequency regime is non-superradiant, see Section
15
, then, in view of Proposition
6.4
, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.244)

-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}

where
E_{0}>0
is a sufficiently large constant, and then we sum in equation (
16.243
) to conclude the proof of Proposition
16.8
.
∎

16.8.
Multipliers for the
\omega
dominated frequency regime
F_{\sharp}

In view of the definition of the frequency regimes, see Definition
15.1
, this frequency regime is manifestly non-superradiant.

The main Proposition of this Section is the following

Proposition 16.9
.

Let
l>0

(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
,
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\alpha>0
. For any

(16.245)

\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in F_{\sharp}(\omega_{high},\lambda_{low},\alpha)
there exists a sufficiently regular function
y(r^{\star})
that satisfies the uniform bound

(16.246)

|y|+|y^{\prime}|\leq B(\omega_{\textit{high}},\lambda_{\textit{low}})

such that for all smooth solutions
u
of Carter’s radial ode (
6.25
) satisfying the outgoing boundary conditions (
6.28
), we have

(16.247)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\Big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2}+1)|u|^{2}\Big)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq B\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{H}u)\right|+\left|\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\imaginary(\bar{H}u)\right|\right)dr^{\star}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\right|dr^{\star}\Big).

We need the following Lemma

Lemma 16.11
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\alpha>0
. For any

(16.248)

\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}
we have

(16.249)

\omega^{2}-V(r)\geq b\omega^{2},\qquad r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}].

Proof.

The potential
V
, of (
6.26
) can be written as

(16.250)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\Delta\left(\frac{r^{3}\frac{d}{dr}\Delta-2r^{2}\Delta+a^{2}r\frac{d}{dr}\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}}+\frac{\Delta a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{4}}+\frac{\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}+\mu_{\textit{KG}}^{2}\cdot\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{\Xi^{2}a^{2}m^{2}+2m\omega a\Xi(\Delta-(r^{2}+a^{2}))}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

We consider the term

(16.251)

\frac{\Delta a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}

of (
16.250
) and we note

(16.252)

\frac{\Delta a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}=\frac{a^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{2Mr}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}a^{2}-\frac{a^{2}r^{2}}{l^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})}=\frac{a^{2}}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2Mr}{r^{2}+a^{2}}-\frac{r^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)<1.

Therefore, we obtain

(16.253)

\omega^{2}-\frac{\Delta a^{2}}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\omega^{2}\geq b\omega^{2}.

By utilizing (
16.253
) that
\omega^{2}+(am)^{2}\geq\lambda_{low}^{-1}|\tilde{\lambda}|
, and (
6.10
) from Lemma
6.1
, we conclude that for
\lambda_{\textit{low}}>0
sufficiently small and
\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
sufficiently large we obtain

(16.254)

\omega^{2}-V\geq b\omega^{2}.

We conclude the result.
∎

We employ a
Q^{y}
current, see Definition
14.1
.

Lemma 16.12
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu_{KG}\geq 0
. Let
\alpha>0
. For any

(16.255)

\lambda^{-1}_{\textit{low}}>0,\qquad\omega_{\textit{high}}>0

both sufficiently large, then for
(\omega,m,\ell)\in\mathcal{F}_{\sharp}(\lambda_{low},\omega_{high},\alpha)
we obtain the following.

There exists a smooth bounded function
y
that satisfies

(16.256)

y^{\prime}\geq b\Delta,\qquad y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\geq b\Delta\omega^{2},\qquad r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}].

Proof.

Let
c_{1}>0
be an arbitrary strictly positive real number. We choose a bounded smooth function
y
such that

(16.257)

\displaystyle y(r)

\displaystyle=(r-r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}})\cdot\frac{dy}{dr}(r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}})+\mathcal{O}(r-r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}})^{2},\quad\text{in a small neightborhood of }r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}

\displaystyle\frac{dy}{dr}

\displaystyle>c_{1}>0,\quad r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}],

where
r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}
is the unique critical point of
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
. Now, by utilizing the multiplier
y
constructed in (
16.257
), we obtain

(16.258)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(\frac{dy}{dr}(\omega^{2}-V)-y\frac{dV}{dr}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(\frac{dy}{dr}(b\omega^{2})-y\frac{dV}{dr}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Delta}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\left(c_{1}b\omega^{2}-y\frac{dV}{dr}\right),

where we used Lemma
16.11
. Finally, note that for the last term of equation (
16.258
), the following holds

(16.259)

\displaystyle-y\frac{dV}{dr}

\displaystyle=-y\frac{d}{dr}\left(V_{\textit{SL}}+\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2m\omega a\Xi)-\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}+V_{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=-y\frac{dV_{\textit{SL}}}{dr}-y\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+a^{2}\omega^{2}-2m\omega a\Xi)-y\frac{d}{dr}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}-y\frac{dV_{\mu_{KG}}}{dr}

\displaystyle\geq-y\frac{dV_{\textit{SL}}}{dr}-y\frac{d}{dr}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2},

where in the last inequality we used that
y
changes sign at the maximum of
\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}
. For
V_{\textit{SL}},V_{\mu}
see Section
6
. Therefore, note that for
\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1},\omega_{\textit{high}}>0
sufficiently large we obtain

(16.260)

c_{1}b\omega^{2}-y\frac{dV}{dr}\geq c_{1}b\omega^{2}-y\frac{d}{dr}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)^{2}\geq b\omega^{2},

after appropriate Young’s inequalities.

We conclude the proof of the Lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
16.9
.

We integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.1
, associated to
Q^{y}
current, to get

(16.261)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}dr^{\star}\left(y^{\prime}|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(y^{\prime}(\omega^{2}-V)-yV^{\prime}\right)|u|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=Q^{y}(\infty)-Q^{y}(-\infty)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}2y\real(u^{\prime}\bar{H})dr^{\star}.

Now, we use (
16.261
), in conjunction with the construction of the

multiplier, see Lemma
16.12
, to obtain

(16.262)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\Big(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+(\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell}+\omega^{2}+1)|u|^{2}\Big)dr^{\star}\leq B\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(2y\real(u^{\prime}H)\Big)dr^{\star}+Q^{y}(\infty)-Q^{y}(-\infty).

Finally, in inequality (
16.262
), in view of Proposition
6.4
, we integrate the energy identity of Lemma
14.4
associated with the current

(16.263)

-E_{0}Q^{K^{+}}-E_{0}Q^{\bar{K}^{+}}

where
E_{0}
is a sufficiently large constant, and we sum it in (
16.262
) to conclude the desired result.

16.10.
Proof of Theorem
16.1

Now, it is immediate to prove the following

Proof of Theorem
16.1
.

We use the results of Propositions
16.1
,
16.2
,
16.3
,
16.4
,
16.5
,
16.6
,
16.7
,
16.8
,
16.9
and choose any
\mathcal{C}>0
such that

(16.265)

\mathcal{C}\geq\lambda_{low}^{-1}\omega^{2}_{high},

and obtain

(16.266)

\displaystyle 1_{\left((\omega,m,\ell)\not{\in}\{\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{m=0\}\}\cup\mathcal{F}_{dS}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}\right)}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+\left(1+\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+1_{(\mathcal{F}_{\flat}\cap\{m=0\})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|\Psi^{\prime}|^{2}+(\omega^{2}+\tilde{\lambda})|\Psi|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}+\int_{r^{\star}_{-\infty}}^{r^{\star}_{\infty}}1_{|m|>0}\left(|u|^{2}+\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+1_{\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\natural}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta\left(|u^{\prime}|^{2}+|u|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{r_{\textit{trap}}}{r}\right)^{2}\left(\tilde{\lambda}+\omega^{2}\right)|u|^{2}\right)dr^{\star}

\displaystyle+B1_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}}|\omega-\omega_{+}m||\omega-\bar{\omega}_{+}m||u|^{2}(-\infty),

where for the set
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{SF},\mathcal{C}}
see (
6.48
). We conclude the proof.

16.11.
Trapping parameters for fixed azimuthal frequency
m

The following lemma is used in the fixed azimuthal frequency Proposition
9.1
. Note that in the following lemma we study trapping for a fixed azimuthal number
m
.

We emphasize that the parameters
\omega_{high},\lambda_{low}
that will be displayed in the following lemma are different from the choices of Section
16.9
.

Lemma 16.13
.

Let
l>0
,
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
and
\mu^{2}_{\textit{KG}}\geq 0
. Let the azimuthal frequency

(16.267)

|m|\geq 0

be fixed.

Then, we obtain that for any

(16.268)

\lambda_{\textit{low}}>0,\qquad\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}>0

both sufficiently small there exists an

(16.269)

\omega_{\textit{high}}(m,\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\lambda_{\textit{low}})>0

sufficiently large such that the following holds

(16.270)

r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}-\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}\leq r_{\textit{trap}}(\omega,m,\ell)\leq r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}+\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},

where
r_{\Delta,\textit{frac}}
is the value of the unique maximum of

(16.271)

\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}.

Proof.

Recall that trapping can only occur in the frequency regimes

(16.272)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\tilde{\lambda}\geq\left(\frac{|a|\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+\alpha\right)^{-1}\omega_{\textit{high}}\}\cap\{am\omega\in\Big(0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{\bar{r}_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}\Big)\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\natural}

\displaystyle=\{(\omega,m,\ell)\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu:\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu|\omega|\geq\omega_{\textit{high}},\mskip 4.0mu plus 2.0mu minus 4.0mu\lambda_{\textit{low}}\tilde{\lambda}\leq\omega^{2}+(am)^{2}\leq\lambda_{\textit{low}}^{-1}\tilde{\lambda}\}\cap\{am\omega\not{\in}\Big[0,\frac{a^{2}m^{2}\Xi}{r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}}+|a|\alpha\tilde{\lambda}\Big)\},

where the above frequency regimes where first defined in Section
15
.

Moreover, recall that
r_{\textit{trap}}
is the maximum (when it is not
0
) of the potential
V
in the frequency regimes
\mathcal{F}_{\textit{dS}},\penalty\ \mathcal{F}_{\natural}
respectively. The derivative of the potential is

(16.273)

\frac{dV}{dr}=\frac{dV_{\textit{SL}}}{dr}+\frac{dV_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}}{dr}+\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

with

(16.274)

\displaystyle\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\left(\tilde{\lambda}-2m\omega a\Xi\right)-\frac{4ram\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\lambda}\left(\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{2m\omega a\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)-\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}}\frac{4ram\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)\right)

and the derivative terms
\frac{dV_{\textit{SL}}}{dr}+\frac{dV_{\mu_{\textit{KG}}}}{dr}
do not depend on the frequencies
(\omega,m,\ell)
.

Therefore, for fixed azimuthal frequency
m
and for any sufficiently small

(16.275)

\epsilon_{\textit{trap}}>0,

we note that by taking

(16.276)

\omega_{\textit{high}}(m,\epsilon_{\textit{trap}},\lambda_{\textit{low}})>0

sufficiently large then the equation

(16.277)

\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Delta}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{2m\omega a\Xi}{\tilde{\lambda}}\right)-\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}}\frac{4ram\Xi}{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}\left(\omega-\frac{am\Xi}{r^{2}+a^{2}}\right)=0

attains a unique solution in the interval
(r_{\Delta,frac}-\epsilon_{trap},r_{\Delta,frac}+\epsilon_{trap})
. Therefore, the function
\frac{dV_{0}}{dr}
attains a unique zero in the desired interval.
∎

Appendix A
Connectedness of the black hole parameter space
\mathcal{B}_{l}

We restate here the lemma we prove in the present Section

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
l>0
. Then, the set
\mathcal{B}_{l}
, see Definition
2.2
, is connected.

Proof.

We fix
l>0
. The necessary and sufficient condition for the quartic polynomial
\Delta
to attain four distinct real roots can be inferred from
[
49
]
to be the following two

(A.1)

\displaystyle-16\left(\frac{a^{6}}{l^{6}}\right)-8\left(\frac{a^{4}}{l^{4}}\right)\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{2}+36\left(\frac{M^{2}a^{2}}{l^{4}}\right)\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-27\frac{M^{4}}{l^{4}}-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{4}+\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{3}>0,

(A.2)

|a|\leq l,\penalty\ \textMu>0.

We rewrite (
A.1
) as follows

(A.3)

\displaystyle P\left(\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)\dot{=}

\displaystyle-27\left(\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{2}+\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\left(36\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)+\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{3}\right)-16\left(\frac{a^{6}}{l^{6}}\right)-8\left(\frac{a^{4}}{l^{4}}\right)\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)^{4}>0,

We write

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{l}

\displaystyle=\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ 0\leq\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}<1\}\cap\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ P(\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})>0\}

\displaystyle=\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ P(\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}})>0\},

where the last equality in (
A.4
) is proved as follows. Suppose that
\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\geq 1
. It is immediate that for any
\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}>0
we have
P(\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}})<0
, since the second term of
P(\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}})
is strictly negative. This concludes the last equality of (
A.4
).

Since
P\left(\frac{(-a)^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)=P\left(\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}}\right)
we conclude that the set
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is symmetric with respect to the line
\{a=0\}
. Therefore, in order to prove that
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is connected it suffices to prove that the set

(A.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}

\displaystyle=\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ P(\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}},\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}})>0\}.

is connected. The reader is refered to the following Figure
5

Figure 5.
The shaded region is
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{l}

The remaining of the proof focuses on proving that the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}
is connected. In view of the identifications

(A.6)

y=\frac{M^{2}}{l^{2}},\qquad x=\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}

we write

(A.7)

P(x,y)=-27y^{2}+y\left(36x(1-x)+(1-x)^{3}\right)-16x^{3}-8x^{2}(1-x)^{2}-x(1-x)^{4}.

We have that for
x=0
and
0<y<\frac{1}{27}
then
P(y,0)>0
.

Before proving that the set (
A.5
) is connected we need a preliminary result for the polynomial
P(x,y)
for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. In the radial directions

(A.8)

y=rx

for fixed
r
the polynomial
P(x,y)
can be written as

(A.9)

\displaystyle P_{r}(x)=P(rx,x)

\displaystyle=rx-27(rx)^{2}+(-1+33rx)x+(-4-33rx)x^{2}+(-6-rx)x^{3}-4x^{4}-x^{5}

\displaystyle=x\left(r-1+(-4+33r-27r^{2})x+(-6-33r)x^{2}+(-4-r)x^{3}-x^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\dot{=}x\tilde{P}_{r}(x).

Now, we will prove that for

(A.10)

r\in(0,\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})]

we have that
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)\leq 0
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and for any

(A.11)

r>\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})

the polynomial
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)
attains exactly two distinct real roots and two complex conjugate roots and
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)>0
in between the real roots.

We study the discriminant of the quartic polynomial
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)
, see
[
49
]
, which we compute it to be

(A.12)

\text{disc}=-4r^{3}\left(-256+9r(32+3r)\right)^{3}.

We directly calculate that for

(A.13)

r\in\left(0,\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})\right)

then
\text{disc}>0
and for

(A.14)

r\in\left(\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3}),+\infty\right)

then
\text{disc}<0
. We have that
0<\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})<1
.

Moreover we have the following auxilliary functions

(A.15)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle=8(-1)(-6-33r)-3(4+r)^{2}=-3(4+(-58+r)r)

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=64(-1)^{3}(r-1)-16(-1)^{2}(-6-33r)^{2}+16(-1)(-4-r)^{2}(-6-33r)-16(-1)^{2}(-4-r)(-4+33r-27r^{2})-3(4+r)^{4}

\displaystyle=r(4096-3r(4864+(-16+r)r))

We immediately calculate that
P,D
cannot both be negative for
r\in(0,1)
.

By the conditions for the nature of the roots of quartic polynomials, see
[
49
]
, we note that for

(A.16)

r\in\left(0,\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})\right)

since
\text{disc}>0
and that one of
P,D
is positive then
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)<0
. For
r=\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})
the polynomial
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)
attains a double real root and two complex conjugate roots and
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)\leq 0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Now for

(A.17)

r\in\left(\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3}),+\infty\right)

since
\text{disc}<0
then the polynomial
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)
attains two distinct real roots and two complex conjugate roots. Therefore, since
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)
is a polynomial of degree
4
then we conclude that
\tilde{P}_{r}(x)>0
in between these two real roots.

Therefore, we have proved that for

(A.18)

r\in\big(0,\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})\big]

the following holds
P_{r}(x)\leq 0
for all
x
and for

(A.19)

r>\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3})

the polynomial
P_{r}(x)
attains exactly two real roots

(A.20)

x_{1}(r)\leq 0<x_{2}(r)

and it is positive between the roots. Note that
x_{1}(1)=0
. It is necessary that the following holds
x_{1}(r)\leq 0
because otherwise no neighborhood of
(0,0)
belongs in the subextremal set
\tilde{B}_{l}
.

Now, we are ready to prove that the set (
A.5
) is connected. A preliminary observation that will help is that part of the boundary of the set (
A.5
) is the following

(A.21)

B=\text{image}(\gamma_{2})

where

(A.22)

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}:[\frac{16}{9}(-3+2\sqrt{3}),+\infty)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\mapsto(rx_{2}(r),x_{2}(r)).

The set
B
is connected because the curve
\gamma_{2}
is uniformly continuous.

Now, we consider the following (nonempty) cylinder around the set
B
and inside
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}

(A.23)

\displaystyle B_{\epsilon}=\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ d\left((x,y),B\right)\leq\epsilon\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}.

Since the boundary
B
is connected then for a sufficiently small
\epsilon
the set
B_{\epsilon}
is also connected.

Now, take two points

(A.24)

(a_{1},M_{1}),\penalty\ (a_{2},M_{2})\in\{(a,M)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}:\penalty\ P(x,y)>0\}

which we rewrite respectively as

(A.25)

(r_{1}b_{1},b_{1}),\penalty\ (r_{2}b_{2},b_{2})

for two
r_{1},r_{2}>0
. Necessarily we have that

(A.26)

x_{1}(r_{1})\leq b_{1}\leq x_{2}(r_{1}),\qquad x_{1}(r_{2})\leq b_{2}\leq x_{2}(r_{2})

Let
\tilde{\epsilon}(\epsilon)>0
be sufficiently small. The line connecting

(A.27)

(r_{1}b_{1},b_{1}),

to

(A.28)

\left(r_{1}\cdot(x_{2}(r_{1})-\tilde{\epsilon}),x_{2}(r_{1})-\tilde{\epsilon}\right)\in B_{\epsilon}

lies entirely in
\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Furthermore, the line connecting

(A.29)

(r_{2}b_{2},b_{2})

to

(A.30)

(r_{2}\cdot(x_{2}(r_{2})-\tilde{\epsilon}),x_{2}(r_{2})-\tilde{\epsilon})\in B_{\epsilon}

lies entirely in
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}
. Since the set
B_{\epsilon}
is connected we conclude that the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}_{l}}
, see (
A.5
), is connected which, as discussed previously, concludes that the set
\mathcal{B}_{l}
is connected.
∎

Appendix B
Geodesic flow of Kerr–de Sitter and
\partial_{t}
-orthogonal trapped null geodesics

The purpose of this Section is to prove Proposition
B.1
, which has no analogue in the asymptotically flat
\Lambda=0
Kerr case.

We define trapped null geodesics as follows

Definition B.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
. Moreover, let
(t,r,\theta,\varphi)
be the Boyer–Lindquist coordinates, see Section
2.3
. Then, a geodesic
\gamma(v)
, parametrized by an affine time
v
, is called trapped null if it satisfies
g(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})=0
and moreover

(B.1)

\lim_{v\rightarrow\infty}r(\gamma(v))\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}).

Note the following proposition

Proposition B.1
.

Let
l>0
and
(a,M)\in\mathcal{B}_{l}
be subextremal Kerr–de Sitter black hole parameters such that

(B.2)

\max_{r\in[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\leq 1,\quad|a|\not{=}0.

Then, there exists a trapped null geodesic

(B.3)

\gamma(v)=(t(v),r_{\Delta,\textit{max}},\theta_{0},\phi(v))

with
g(\dot{\gamma},\partial_{t})=0
, for some
\theta_{0}\in[0,\pi]
, where
r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
is the unique maximum of
\Delta
, see Lemma
3.4
.

Finally, if (
B.2
) does not hold, then there exists no
\partial_{t}
orthogonal trapped null geodesic in the Kerr–de Sitter background
\mathcal{M}
.

Remark B.1
.

Note that if (
B.2
) holds, then the ergoregion is connected, see Section
2.4
.

Note that in the asympotically flat Kerr case there exists no trapped null geodesic with zero
\partial_{t}
energy, see
[
17
]
. This non-existence of a
\partial_{t}
orthogonal trapped null geodesic, in the
\Lambda=0
Kerr exterior, was crucial in the proof of rigidity of Kerr in a neighborhood of Kerr, see
[
1
]
.

First, note the following Lemma for the null geodesic flow of Kerr–de Sitter

Lemma B.1
.

A curve
\gamma=(t,r,\theta,\phi)
on the Kerr–de Sitter background, parametrized by a parameter
v
, is a null geodesic if and only if it satisfies

(B.4)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\dot{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{a\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\left(\mathcal{E}a\sin^{2}\theta-\Xi\mathcal{L}\right)+\Xi^{2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}a-(r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{E}\right)}{\Delta}

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\dot{\phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{a\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{\mathcal{E}a\sin^{2}\theta-\Xi\mathcal{L}}{\sin^{2}\theta}+\Xi^{2}\frac{a\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}a-(r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{E}\right)}{\Delta}

\displaystyle\rho^{4}(\dot{\theta})^{2}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\theta}\mathcal{K}-\Xi^{2}\frac{\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}-a\mathcal{E}\sin^{2}\theta\right)^{2}}{\sin^{2}\theta}

\displaystyle\rho^{4}(\dot{r})^{2}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{2}\left((r^{2}+a^{2})\mathcal{E}-a\Xi\mathcal{L}\right)^{2}-\Delta\mathcal{K},

with
\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,\penalty\ \Delta_{\theta}=1+\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta
see Definition
2.4
, where note we have not fully decoupled the geodesic equations. The constant
\mathcal{K}
is Carter’s constant of motion, and note the conserved quantities

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=g(\dot{\gamma},\partial_{t})=-\left(1-\frac{2Mr}{\rho^{2}}\right)\dot{t}-\frac{2Mra\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho^{2}}\dot{\phi},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=-g(\dot{\gamma},\partial_{\phi})=\frac{2Mra\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho^{2}}\dot{t}-\sin^{2}\theta\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}-a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta\Delta}{\rho^{2}}\dot{\phi},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}

\displaystyle=\rho^{4}\left(\dot{\theta}\right)^{2}+\frac{\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}}{\sin^{2}\theta}-a^{2}E^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}

\displaystyle=\Xi^{2}\left(\mathcal{Q}+a^{2}\mathcal{E}^{2}-2a\Xi\mathcal{L}\mathcal{E}\Xi\right).

Proof.

This proof can be inferred from
[
8
]
or
[
25
]
.
∎

Now we are ready for

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

We substitute

(B.6)

\mathcal{E}=0

in the geodesic equations (
B.4
) and obtain

(B.7)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\dot{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{a\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\left(-\Xi\mathcal{L}\right)+\Xi^{2}\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}a\right)}{\Delta},

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\dot{\phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{a\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\frac{-\Xi\mathcal{L}}{\sin^{2}\theta}+\Xi^{2}\frac{a\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}a\right)}{\Delta},

\displaystyle\rho^{4}(\dot{\theta})^{2}=\Theta(\theta)

\displaystyle\dot{=}\Delta_{\theta}\Xi^{2}\mathcal{Q}-\Xi^{2}\frac{\left(\Xi\mathcal{L}\right)^{2}}{\sin^{2}\theta},

\displaystyle\rho^{4}(\dot{r})^{2}=R(r)

\displaystyle\dot{=}\Xi^{2}\left(-a\Xi\mathcal{L}\right)^{2}-\Delta\Xi^{2}\mathcal{Q}=a^{2}\Xi^{2}\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}-\Delta\Xi^{2}\mathcal{Q}=a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}\right).

Let
r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}
be the value where
\Delta
attains its unique maximum and choose
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{Q}
such that

(B.8)

\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}=\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}.

We first solve the
\theta
-equation of motion. By taking

(B.9)

0<\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}\leq 1,

the
\theta
-equation of motion can be solved globally

(B.10)

(\dot{\theta})^{2}=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\left(\frac{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta-\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}}{\sin^{2}\theta}\right)\Xi^{2}\mathcal{Q},

where
\Delta_{\theta}=1+\frac{a^{2}}{l^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta
, since there exists a
\theta_{0}\in(0,\pi)
such that

(B.11)

\Delta_{\theta_{0}}\sin^{2}\theta_{0}-\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}=0.

Therefore, for the rest of the proof we assume that
\theta(v)\equiv\theta_{0}
for all
v\in\mathbb{R}
, where
v
is the affine time.

Second, we solve the
r
-equation of motion. We find the value of
r
that the trapped null geodesics will asymptote to. The
r
equations of motion read

(B.12)

\displaystyle R(r)\equiv a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}\right)\equiv a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})-\Delta}{a^{2}}\right)\mathcal{Q}=0,\qquad\frac{dR(r)}{dr}=-\Xi^{2}\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\mathcal{Q}=0.

From the latter equation of (
B.12
) we obtain

(B.13)

\frac{dR}{dr}(r)\Big|_{r=r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}}=0,

where
r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}\in(r_{+},\bar{r}_{+})
. Then, we solve the first equation of (
B.12
). From the known well posedness theorem, the
r
-equation of motion can be solved locally

(B.14)

\displaystyle\rho^{4}(\dot{r})^{2}=a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}-\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\right)\mathcal{Q},\penalty\ \text{or equivalently}\penalty\ \frac{\rho^{2}dr}{\sqrt{a^{2}\Xi^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}-\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\right)\mathcal{Q}}}=\pm dv,

with initial conditions
r(0)=r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}
,
\dot{r}(0)=\dot{r}_{0}
. By (
B.8
) we note that
r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}
is a root of multiplicity
2
of the following

(B.15)

\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}-\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}=\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})-\Delta}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}

and therefore the affine parameter
v
takes values up to infinity since we can write

v-v_{0}=\int_{r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}}^{r(v)}\frac{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos{\theta_0}\right)dr}{\sqrt{R(r)}}

for all
v
in a small interval around
v_{0}
. Therefore, if the curve

(B.16)

\gamma(v)

is a trapped null geodesic, it needs to be complete.

The
t,\phi
equations of motions, namely the first and second equations of respectively (
B.7
), can be solved easily since they are linear.

Therefore, if (
B.9
) holds, then the trapped null geodesic equations (
B.7
) correspond to a complete trapped null geodesic

(B.17)

\gamma=(t(v),r_{\Delta,\textit{max}},\theta_{0},\phi(v))

where for
\theta_{0}
see (
B.11
) with

(B.18)

g(\dot{\gamma},\partial_{t})=0.

Lastly, from the last two equations of motion of (
B.7
) we note that if
\max_{[r_{+},\bar{r}_{+}]}\frac{\Delta}{a^{2}}>1
then there exists no
\partial_{t}
orthogonal trapped null geodesic, in view of the fact that the
r
coordinate of the geodesic needs to be
r_{\Delta,\textit{max}}
. We proceed to prove this by first noting that if the contrary holds, then from the last equation of motion of (
B.7
) we obtain

(B.19)

\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}-\frac{\Delta(r_{\Delta,\textit{max}})}{a^{2}}\mathcal{Q}=0\implies\Xi^{2}\mathcal{L}^{2}>\mathcal{Q}.

Now, we combine (
B.19
) with the second to last equation of motion of (
B.7
) and arrive at a contradiction, since we obtain
(\dot{\theta})^{2}<0
.

We conclude the proof.
∎

Note the following Remark

Remark B.2
.

We note that if we formally identify

(B.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\omega,\qquad\mathcal{L}\rightarrow m,\qquad\mathcal{Q}\rightarrow\lambda^{(a\omega)}_{m\ell},

then the geodesic equation for the motion of
\dot{r}
, see Lemma
B.1
, can be written in the familiar form

(B.21)

\frac{\rho^{4}}{\Xi^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}(\dot{r})^{2}=\mathcal{E}^{2}-V_{0}(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{E},r)

where
V_{0}
is the potential appearing in Carter’s separation of variables, see Proposition
6.2
.
